#6821. This Brand-New Illinois Farmhouse Was Designed to Look Hundreds of Years Old

The team at Buccellato Design and Architecture focused on incorporating heritage details and timeworn furnishings.

For Aimee and Kevin Buccellato, the best compliment they could receive about a project isn’t, “Wow, it looks so different in here.” It’s that a home looks like they haven’t changed a thing.

On a country road in Momence, Illinois, a beloved farmhouse stands as a timeworn landmark for the surrounding agricultural community. For the homeowners—a retired couple returning to their Midwestern roots—the property was as much a repository of memories as it was a place to create new ones. “Having grown up nearby, the property evoked a strong sense of memory and place,” says Aimee, who founded Buccellato Design and Architecture alongside her husband, Kevin. “At the outset of the project, and throughout, our clients wanted the new house to feel like it had always been there, so much so that perhaps the neighbors could be led to believe that the original house remained intact, yet amended by skillful renovation and discreet additions.”

It was an architectural sleight of hand rooted in a distinctly Midwestern philosophy: “Be good to the neighbors.” Alongside the Buccellatos, the couple approached the 6,000-square-foot rebuild with the restraint of preservationists and the imagination of storytellers. “Great care was taken to instill the home and outbuildings with a sense of history and rootedness, creating a home that feels as though it’s been part of this bucolic setting for centuries,” says Kevin of the project, which also included the restoration of the property’s original 19th-century barn and corn crib. “We drew inspiration from traditional farm complexes, where homesteads were added slowly over time, reconnecting the site with its Folk-Victorian-Farm-House and agricultural building roots.”

Inside, the narrative continues through antique white oak floors, salvaged beams, heritage florals, and rooms that borrow their palettes from the landscape. In the kitchen, a wall of windows replaces upper cabinetry, framing sunrise over the barn and walnut trees. The glass porch offers sweeping views of the Kankakee River, while a tucked-away speakeasy recalls the original farmhouse’s stone foundation.

For Kevin and Aimee, the guiding principle was a new construction home that felt “sensitive, not wasteful, polite, not ostentatious,” an exercise in knowing when architecture and interiors should defer to their surroundings. The result is a house with an invented past and a very real sense of belonging that does the Midwest—and the neighbors—proud.

FAST FACTSDesigner: Buccellato Design and ArchitectureStylist: Cate RaganLocation: Momence, IllinoisThe Space: A four-bedroom, four-and-a-half-bathroom home across 6,000 square feet

The entryway offers a fitting introduction to the home'’s carefully constructed sense of history, replete with nickel gap paneling, fumed and waxed antique white oak floors, and a light-filled staircase. Even the furnishings—including a vintage Persian rug and antique Jacobean gateleg table—suggest a collection acquired over generations.

Benjamin Moore’s Nelson Blue sets a serene backdrop for the sitting room, where traditional silhouettes and understated patterns make an easy pairing. “This room was designed to honor the history of this property and embody the love that our clients hold for this special place,” adds Aimee.

A tufted mohair sofa, realized in a rich umber, lends a sense of history, while an iconic feather lamp by Bunny Williams offers a reminder that even the most classic rooms benefit from a little irreverence.

In the kitchen, the Buccellatos traded upper cabinets for an expansive wall of windows overlooking the farmland, relocating the practical necessities to an adjacent dish room and larder. Salvaged white oak beams and barnboards overhead reinforce the home’s agricultural roots, while furniture-like cabinetry and leathered black quartzite countertops strike a timeless note.

With natural light pouring in from three sides, the dining room is what the Buccellatos describe as “peaceful, soft, and ethereal,” equally suited to both morning coffee and candlelit dinners. A dreamy mural wallpaper by Susan Harter references the property’s location along the Kankakee River, while formal seating lends a sense of occasion without sacrificing the home’s easygoing country charm.

Nestled among mature trees, the primary bedroom feels almost suspended in the surrounding landscape. The Buccellatos leaned into that sense of serenity with the palette. “It’s a dreamlike mix of earthy neutrals and pale blues that paints a serene backdrop for elegant details, vintage furnishings, and a delicate mix of subtle pattern,” adds Kevin.

A French country canopy bed, dressed in tailored Schumacher linen, takes center stage, while vintage-inspired furnishings and subtle pattern keep the room from feeling overly precious. An adjacent sitting room offers a place to read the morning paper or unwind with a cup of tea in the evenings.

In the vestibule, a little pattern goes a long way. A climbing floral from Schumacher in inky charcoal makes a charming counterpoint to the bedroom’s hushed palette, while a checked window bench is the ideal spot to read, giving the passageway purpose.

Pale blue walls and creamy millwork lend the primary bathroom an old-fashioned prettiness. A custom vanity introduces a tailored architectural element, while a Cowtan & Tout floral Roman shade softens the room’s more utilitarian details.

Designed as a playful respite for guests and future grandchildren, this bedroom plays off pattern and primary hues. “The red painted woodwork feels unexpected and cheerful against the blue-and-white wallpaper, striped rug, and patterned bedding,” says Aimee.

“The brick-clad main block echoes the footprint of the original farmhouse on this site, yet the story we wrote for the house begins with the folk Victorian wing, inspired by L-shaped farmhouses of that period,” says Kevin. “In our modern narrative, the clapboard-sided wing came first, followed by the brick-clad ’addition,’ which takes inspiration from the later Colonial Revival period in its scale and sophistication of detailing.”

Founded by Aimee and Kevin Buccellato, Buccellato Design & Architecture is a practice marked by twenty-plus years of close collaboration, in architectural practice and in life. With projects on both coasts and throughout the country, the firm’s award-winning work reveals a dedication to craft, artistry, and detail. Achieved in partnership with clients, their projects reflect a purposeful sense of rootedness in the landscape and abiding devotion to beauty and the comfort of home.

#6820. Cisco Intros Privileged Infrastructure Access for People and AI Agents

Cisco has introduced Privileged Infrastructure Access, an approach to infrastructure administration that gives people and AI agents temporary, task-specific permissions instead of leaving standing privileges available after their work is done.

In a recent blog post, Cisco's Matt Caulfield described a system that uses cryptographic identity to connect an administrator or agent to the infrastructure needed for a particular task. Access is governed by short-lived privileges, with session recording providing a record of the work performed.

"If there is no work being done, no privilege exists," Caulfield wrote. Cisco positions the offering as a way to bring identity controls directly into infrastructure operations while reducing dependence on static credentials, shared secrets, and broad access.

The announcement follows Cisco's previously announced Teleport partnership and its Infrastructure Identity concept, which connects the identity of a person or machine with the resources it can access, the actions it performs, and the permissions governing those actions.

Caulfield described a gap between the controls organizations apply to workforce applications and those used to administer network infrastructure. Companies have invested in single sign-on, multifactor authentication, and risk-based controls for application access, he wrote, but those protections often do not follow an engineer logging into a router or switch.

Instead, infrastructure access may depend on long-lived credentials, shared accounts, or separate policy systems. According to Cisco, that separation makes it harder to enforce least privilege and establish which person or system performed a particular action.

The company illustrates the problem with an administrator updating hundreds of switches during a maintenance window. If some updates fail, the administrator needs to troubleshoot before the window closes. The permissions available to handle that work, however, may cover more infrastructure and remain active longer than the task requires.

Traditional privileged access management can reduce risk through credential vaulting and rotation, Caulfield wrote. But he argued that operational friction can undermine those controls when teams create exceptions to complete urgent work. Those exceptions can leave credentials and privileges usable after the immediate need has passed.

Cisco's stated objective is to give infrastructure teams a consistent route to the devices they manage while providing security teams with policy enforcement, temporary permissions, and a record of activity. The company describes operational convenience as part of the design, rather than treating access governance solely as a credential-management problem.

Privileged Infrastructure Access makes access an on-demand action, according to the announcement. Cryptographic identity establishes the actor, and the resulting connection provides only the privileges needed to complete the task. Those privileges expire when the task concludes.

For customers using Cisco Identity Services Engine (ISE) and Terminal Access Controller Access-Control System Plus (TACACS+), the offering can draw on existing policy and device context to govern access to Cisco routers and switches. Cisco says customers can extend the same access model to other infrastructure, including servers, Kubernetes clusters, and cloud resources.

#6818. Survey Finds 84% of Organizations Reported at Least One AI-Related Incident in the Past Year

Companies are running into problems as they give artificial intelligence more control over critical business applications, with 84% reporting at least one AI-related incident in the past year, according to a new survey from identity security firm Delinea.

The company's 2026 Application Access Governance report found that some organizations have experienced incorrect financial transactions and AI actions they couldn't trace or explain. The findings raise concerns about whether enterprises have enough oversight of AI systems that can make decisions and take actions on their own

The findings are based on an independent survey of 1,000 senior finance and finance technology leaders in the United States and United Kingdom. The AI-specific analysis included 879 respondents whose organizations use the technology in critical business applications.

The report found that 35% of surveyed organizations have deployed AI agents capable of taking actions within business applications. However, only 11% of organizations using AI said their governance and security controls were keeping pace with deployment.

Delinea also found gaps in centralized visibility into agent deployment and permissions, including who owns agents and who approved their use.

Some respondents reported segregation-of-duties conflicts involving AI, while others identified agents that continued operating after the employees who deployed them had left the organization.

The research also highlighted gaps in monitoring access across business systems. Only 46% of respondents said they continuously monitor and manage cumulative access risks across applications.

Those gaps can make it difficult to identify segregation-of-duties conflicts, in which a person or agent has permissions across systems that should be kept separate.

Despite those issues, 95% of respondents said they were confident their access controls would withstand scrutiny in an external audit.

However, 26% reported access-related audit findings that had recurred in consecutive years, while 25% said some findings had remained unresolved for three or more years.

Only 22% of organizations had a single named owner for each application, with ownership tracked in one system of record.

Meanwhile, 60% reported an increase over the past two years in the number of critical applications operating outside the scope of access governance or Sarbanes-Oxley Act compliance.

The findings highlight the oversight challenges associated with giving AI agents authority to act within business systems, particularly when organizations cannot consistently identify who owns an agent or trace its actions.

Frank Vukovits, chief security scientist at Delinea, said organizations remain responsible for the actions AI agents take within their systems.

"When an agent can approve a transaction or change permissions, the business owns the outcome. You need to know what every agent can do, who owns it, and who approved it, before an auditor asks or your financials are misstated," Vukovits said.

For the full report, go to the Delinea site.

#6817. All of Princess Kate’s Remembrance Sunday Looks Over the Years

The Princess of Wales first attended the event in 2011

Princess Catherine has joined the royal family for the National Service of Remembrance every year since 2011, the same year that she got married to Prince William. The somber annual ceremony, held on Remembrance Sunday at the Cenotaph, a war memorial in London, honors service members who have lost their lives at war. It takes place on the second Sunday of November, and it involves a church service, a wreath-laying ceremony, and a formal procession, all of which are attended by various members of the British royal family.

When it comes to her royal style, the Princess of Wales has long embraced a more sustainable approach, frequently rewearing dresses, jewelry, and accessories. Over the years, that style philosophy has been on full display at Remembrance Sunday, where Kate has made a tradition of recycling looks and adding sentimental touches through meaningful brooches and heirloom jewelry.

One of her signature accessories is the red poppy pin, a symbol that has come to be widely associated with the holiday. Kate is often seen wearing three poppies at once, and while the royal family has never confirmed the significance behind the number, one theory suggests it may be a personal nod to her great-grandmother’s three brothers, all of whom lost their lives during World War I.

Throughout the years, Kate has also worn pieces that once belonged to late royal family members like Queen Elizabeth II and Princess Diana, which she has styled with outfits from Catherine Walker, Alexander McQueen, Temperley London, Diane von Furstenberg, and more.

Ahead, take a look back at Princess Kate’s Remembrance Sunday style through the years, from her first appearance in 2011 to now. And be sure to check back for more updates once the Princess of Wales steps out for the 2026 ceremony, too.

For her first appearance with the royal family at the Cenotaph memorial, Kate wore a black Diane von Furstenberg coat adorned with a double poppy pin along with a Jane Corbett hat and Kiki McDonough earrings.

At the 2012 Remembrance Sunday ceremony, the then-Duchess of Cambridge rewore her Diane von Furstenberg coat from the previous year, this time paired with a Philip Treacy hat, Annoushka earrings, and a single sparkling poppy brooch.

Opting for a different outerwear look for her third appearance, Kate wore a single-breasted Temperley London coat, which she accessorized with a Sylvia Fletcher for Lock & Co. hat and Kiki McDonough hoop earrings with Annoushka pearl drops.

In 2014, Kate wore a black Alexander McQueen jacket over a black ruffled BCBG dress. She rewore the Jane Corbett hat she donned in 2011, and she added a floral brooch, a Mappin & Webb diamond necklace, and Princess Diana’s sapphire earrings.

Kate stepped out in another Alexander McQueen coat for the 2015 Remembrance Day service, which featured velvet trim along the collar, sleeves, and waist. The then-duchess topped it off with a Lock & Co. hat adorned with a sculptural butterfly and black veil, the Kiki McDonough hoop earrings and Annoushka pearl drops she’d worn in 2013, and a triple-poppy pin on her lapel.

In another rewear, Kate donned the same Diane von Furstenberg coat she wore to her first and second Remembrance Sunday services in 2011 and 2012. This time, she styled it with a John Boyd hat, diamond-and-pearl earrings, and three poppies on her lapel.

Kate switched up her look for the 2017 National Service of Remembrance, opting for a double-breasted, collarless Dolce & Gabbana coat with oversized gold buttons, statement Oscar de la Renta earrings in gold, and a Philip Treacy hat she wore in 2006, during her early days of dating Prince William. She also debuted a new hair look, pinning it up to create the illusion of a short bob.

The 2018 Remembrance Day Service marked a significant milestone: the 100th anniversary of the end of World War I. For the occasion, Kate wore a custom Alexander McQueen jacket with military-inspired details, including a high white collar, red shoulder embellishments, and crest-style buttons. She completed the look with a Lock & Co. hat, a triple red poppy pin, and a brooch honoring the women who served during World War I.

Opting for another military-inspired jacket, Kate wore a Catherine Walker coat and a Philip Treacy hat featuring a black veil. Forgoing her usual poppy pin, she instead wore a special pin known as the “Codebreaker’s Brooch” in honor of her grandmother who worked at Bletchley Park during World War II.

At the socially distanced 2020 Remembrance Day ceremony, held amid the COVID-19 pandemic, Kate once again opted for a military-inspired look. She wore a Catherine Walker jacket with fringe detailing at the shoulders, complete with a Philip Treacy fascinator, pearl drop earrings, and her signature triple-poppy pin.

Kate brought back her Alexander McQueen coat from the 2018 service for the 2021 Remembrance Day ceremony. She paired the repeat look with Princess Diana’s pearl earrings, a wide-brimmed Lock & Co. hat, and her signature triple-poppy pin, along with a crystal flower brooch from the Royal British Legion.

For her first Remembrance Day Service following Queen Elizabeth II’s passing, the Princess of Wales wore a black Catherine Walker coat, Princess Diana’s pearl-and-diamond earrings, and a wide-brimmed Philip Treacy hat. She completed the look with her signature trio of poppies, paired with an Art Deco-inspired diamond brooch.

The Princess of Wales opted for multiple rewears at the 2023 Remembrance Sunday service. She repeated the custom Catherine Walker coat she first wore in 2019 and the Philip Treacy hat she previously wore at the 2012 and 2017 services. Then, for some added sparkle, she wore Queen Elizabeth’s diamond-and-pearl earrings, and her signature three-poppy pin.

Kate didn’t make many public appearances in 2024 due to her cancer diagnosis, but she still attended the Remembrance Sunday service that year. She wore a Catherine Walker coat dress, featuring a velvet bow collar, and she accessorized with a Juliette Botterill hat, Queen Elizabeth’s earrings, and her triple-poppy pin.

Kate’s look at the 2025 Remembrance Sunday service featured a rewear with a new twist. She opted for the same Catherine Walker coat from 2022, but this time, she styled it with a lace navy-blue pie-crust collar blouse draping out of the top. She accessorized with a sculptural Lock & Co. fascinator, Princess Diana’s pearl-drop earrings, a single poppy brooch, and a Royal Air Force brooch.

#6815. Report: Cyber Criminals Move Toward More Targeted Phishing Attacks

Cyber criminals are moving away from mass phishing scams and focusing on more targeted attacks, increasingly using trusted websites and services to trick victims, according to a new report from KnowBe4. The security firm found that spear-phishing attacks increased 41% over the past year.

The company's Phishing Threat Trends Report, Vol. 8 found that overall phishing volume increased 29.8% year over year. Organizations received an average of one dangerous e-mail for every 733 messages, but the bigger concern is how attackers are changing their approach.

Rather than sending the same fraudulent message to thousands of people, criminals are spending more time researching their targets and creating convincing messages that appear to come from trusted coworkers, businesses, and technology platforms.

The findings are based on threat data from KnowBe4 Defend, the company's inbound e-mail security platform, which processes approximately 1.15 billion event signals daily.

KnowBe4 found that several traditional phishing methods declined significantly compared with 2025. Mobile-focused scams dropped 47.3%, advance-fee fraud fell 31.3%, and missed-message scams declined 11.3%.

Meanwhile, spear phishing increased 41.1%, pre-attack reconnaissance rose 33.7%, and company impersonation climbed 28%.

The shift suggests that attackers are finding more value in carefully crafted scams that exploit existing business relationships and workplace routines.

Business e-mail compromise (BEC) remains particularly common, accounting for 51.2% of phishing e-mails analyzed in the report. Human resources, finance departments, and executive leadership are among the most frequently targeted groups.

Attackers also appear to be timing their campaigns around normal business activity. KnowBe4 found that 90.4% of phishing messages arrive Monday through Friday, with Monday accounting for the largest share at 20.6%.

One of the report's more troubling findings involves the growing use of legitimate technology platforms to deliver malicious content.

KnowBe4 found that phishing attacks were twice as likely to originate from legitimate infrastructure in the third quarter of 2026 compared with the previous quarter.

That includes AI services, where attackers are taking advantage of trusted domains to host fake developer consoles, login prompts, and credential-stealing pages. The report also identified a 228% increase in AI-brand impersonation attacks between March and August.

Because these malicious pages can operate on legitimate domains with valid security certificates, users may not see the usual warning signs associated with suspicious websites.

"For years, security advice has focused on a predictable checklist: Check the sender address, hover over the link, and look for the padlock," said Jack Chapman, senior vice president of threat intelligence at KnowBe4.

"While still foundational, that guidance is becoming less useful by the day."

The findings echo a recent report about management software and AI e-mail assistants. Research from Fortra and Barracuda showed how criminals are using legitimate administrative tools and hidden AI instructions to manipulate users and compromise systems.

Attackers are exploiting enterprises' own artificial intelligence systems and extending pressure beyond internal networks to employees and their families, according to a new Semperis survey of organizations that suffered disruptive ransomware attacks.

A new study from consulting firm KPMG found that while AI investment continues to grow, relatively few organizations consistently measure whether the technology's benefits justify its costs.

In an active social engineering campaign, cyber attackers are impersonating IT help desks and using fake passkey setup requests to compromise employee identities and gain access to enterprise cloud data.

Artificial intelligence is saving scientists significant amounts of time, but researchers are giving some of those gains back by checking the technology's work, according to a new study involving Google, Google DeepMind, and MIT.

#6814. Study: Sign-In Problems Are Primary Drivers of Help Desk Tickets

Sign-in problems account for up to 53% of help desk tickets at individual enterprises, but network issues can take significantly longer to resolve, according to a new study from ControlUp.

Analyzing 1,069,110 help desk tickets, the IT operations company identified password resets, account lockouts, and multifactor authentication (MFA) issues as major drivers of support demand.

At one large enterprise, account-related tickets took a median of 11 minutes to resolve, compared with 50 hours for network problems. Meanwhile, 87% of network and phone tickets were reassigned between teams, compared with 20% of account tickets.

MFA enrollment and re-enrollment problems appeared at 40% of organizations, with one in five linking these issues to employees replacing or upgrading their phones. Virtual desktop problems appeared at 55% of organizations analyzed.

Traditional phone support remained a major channel for IT assistance.

Among organizations that recorded how support requests were submitted, 57% reported that phone calls accounted for approximately two-thirds or more of contacts. At one organization, that figure reached 99%.

Machine-generated tickets from monitoring, security, backup, and patching systems appeared at half of the organizations studied. At 15%, automated tickets represented between 25% and 50% of all requests.

The research covered enterprises across retail, healthcare, financial services, manufacturing, construction, energy, and IT services.

ControlUp said the findings highlight the need to evaluate recurring problems and their operational impact rather than relying solely on ticket volume and resolution speed.

"IT organizations have traditionally measured support performance by how quickly they close tickets, but that doesn't tell the whole story," said Simon Townsend, senior vice president of marketing and office of the CTO at ControlUp.

"A password reset that takes a few minutes and an infrastructure failure that disrupts an entire location shouldn't carry the same operational weight."

For more information, go to the ControlUp site.

#6811. Enterprise AI Spending Outpaces Adoption Growth, Report Finds

Enterprise AI spending is growing faster than the number of organizations paying for it, according to a new Menlo Ventures report that points to a shift from buying access for employees to financing work performed by agents.

The venture capital firm's 2026 State of Enterprise AI report estimates deployment spending reached a $176.8 billion annualized run rate by September, up from $33 billion in 2025. The share of enterprises paying for AI rose from 55% to 65%.

A larger bill can reflect broader use inside an organization, but it does not independently establish that the additional work is useful or profitable.

Menlo's methodology describes revenue figures as annualized run rates. The $176.8 billion estimate therefore should not be read as $176.8 billion already spent during the first nine months of 2026.

The report also distinguishes unusually heavy spenders from the typical respondent. Average reported spending reached 4.7 times the prior year's level, while the median reached 2.2 times. That gap suggests a smaller group of large spenders drove the average substantially.

The practical implication is that an industry growth figure may be a poor benchmark for an individual organization's budget. Buyers need to understand their own workload growth and the results it produces.

"Agents are changing the economics of software," Menlo principal Derek Xiao said in the firm's announcement.

Menlo reports that agents account for 80% of tokens on OpenRouter, a service providing access to multiple models. That measures activity on one platform, rather than all enterprise AI use. Token consumption also measures processing volume, rather than completed business outcomes.

An agent that makes repeated attempts can consume more resources without delivering proportionately more value. For procurement teams, the relevant comparison is consequently the cost of completing acceptable work, including the review it requires.

Menlo estimates that Anthropic, OpenAI, and Google capture 69% of enterprise AI deployment spending. That measure is distinct from their shares of enterprise language-model API spending, a narrower category. The figures should not be used interchangeably.

The report draws on a Morning Consult survey of nearly 400 U.S. enterprise decision-makers responsible for more than $4 billion in AI spending. Market totals are modeled estimates informed by surveys, public filings, and third-party reports. Menlo discloses investments in Anthropic and OpenRouter.

A separate announcement from Information Services Group describes buyers putting more emphasis on the economics and reliability of production deployments. ISG says ongoing security and observability work remains necessary as organizations scale.

That provides a useful distinction between market demand and business value. Suppliers can generate growing revenue while customers still determine which deployments justify the cost.

For enterprise leaders, the new spending estimate strengthens the case for examining usage beyond license counts. It leaves the harder purchasing question with the customer: How much worthwhile work did the increased spending actually buy?

Menlo's State of Enterprise AI report is available here.

#6809. Costco's New Porch Goose Feels So '80s Coded, and Fans Are Running to Stores to Find It

Every item on this page was hand-picked by a House Beautiful editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Think of these backyard birds as your new mascot: They can sit on your porch, front yard, or terrace to offer cheerful charm for visitors and passersby. Though porch geese were once popular in the 1980s, they’re making a comeback in a big way—and are even available at Costco for $35. If you ask us, it’s a small price for so much joy.

When most people think about buying home products from Costco, they usually think of strictly practical purchases: Heaps of toilet paper rolls, a shed to conceal patio furniture, or even furniture that can turn a home office into a bedroom on demand. But make no mistake, even Costco is brimming with whimsy.

#6808. With the Hunger Games Press Tour, Have We Reached the Final Frontier of Method Dressing?

The cast has offered a pointed take on the state of today’s red carpets

Another Hunger Games movie, a prequel to the initial series, premieres later this month, and with it comes another press tour full of fantastically far-fetched fashion. As the promotional events begin to stack up for Hunger Games: Sunrise on the Reaping, we are reminded of the familiar lore of this dystopian empire: In a fictionalized future America called Panem, the 12 districts that supply resources to the rich and powerful people of the Capitol are forced to send two children to fight to the death each year. The excess of the Capitol is signaled in the films via outlandish style, which the newest film’s cast members have been attempting to channel in their recent looks. The goal is to be extreme—but in an era of extremes on the red carpet, these over-the-top ensembles actually don’t look so out of place.

Elle Fanning plays a young Effie Trinket, who is essentially a Capitol stylist dressing the young “tributes” in all sorts of fanciful fashions—and doing the same for herself. (In the first film series, the character wore a lot of McQueen.) So far, Fanning’s press looks have deliberately channeled that outlandishness. For a London fan screening, she wore a gown from Robert Wun’s Fall 2026 couture collection that subverted ideas of childlike wonder. Her ensemble began in miniature with a music-box-style ballerina atop a bubblegum pink fascinator. The corresponding human-size gown features a doubled-up bodice, the outer layer appearing to peel off, above a full tulle skirt, all in that same dusty pink.

For her other appearances, Fanning has established a de facto uniform of precisely tailored skirt suits, each paired with some sort of coordinating fascinator tilted just so above her brow. She has selected a soft pink pleated skirt suit from Valentino complete with a ruffled collar, gloves, and a butterfly-trimmed chapeau; a sharp-shouldered version in a deep, almost reptilian satin green from Demna’s Gucci; and a floral, bow-embellished one from Vivienne Westwood. The looks are decidedly feminine in a manner that is at once both futuristic and retro in very Capitol fashion.

Fanning’s stylist Samantha McMillen has been vocal about the references to the character, with captions like “Gucci Effie | @demna puts “The Hunger” into @thehungergames,” and “Elle bringing 🦋 “Sunrise” 🦋 to the Academy Gala.” And it’s not just Fanning doing this either. Costar Mckenna Grace recently recreated a Katniss Everdeen Capitol tour look in which Katniss’s dress quite literally caught fire. Created by Vietnamese designer Cong Tri, who is known for his sculptural silhouettes, the recreation nixed the pyrotechnics but printed graphic flames onto the skirt and bodice. With the help of stylist Molly Dickson, Grace also revived Katniss’s wedding dress with a frothy white, silver-embellished gown by Monique Lhuillier.

I haven’t seen the most recent films, but all of them examine the same concept of this dystopian society propped up by the Hunger Games themselves at different points on a timeline. In the first film specifically, the older Trinket uses their version of a press tour to craft a narrative around Katniss and her fellow tribute Peeta Mellark and win favor among their audience through larger-than-life fashion. The aforementioned fire dress referenced Everdeen’s district’s coal mining industry, and the wedding dress was meant to create the illusion of a love story between the two of them. The series of looks is designed to be projected to the surrounding communities—and garner as much attention as possible. The characters can quite literally benefit later in the life-and-death-style games from the loyalties and sympathies the theatrics spawn.

It’s only in recent years that this idea of method dressing, or theming press tour looks around an actor’s current project, has become commonplace. It began in earnest with the work of Law Roach and Zendaya around the Dune and Spider-Man franchises, Challengers, and The Drama. It was perhaps commercially crystallized in 2023 by Andrew Mukamal and Margot Robbie with Barbie—a press tour that spawned so much digital attention it earned its own coffee-table book—and, more recently, Wuthering Heights. Now, it has proof of concept, attracting stars like Cynthia Erivo and Ariana Grande in their promotion of Wicked, the new cast of Sense and Sensibility, and Lili Reinhart’s rom-com references for The Love Hypothesis. At its most reductive, the tactic is a surefire way to drum up conversation on social media; at its best, a clever way to add depth to a project’s storytelling.

As press tours become more and more oriented towards social media, it’s difficult to isolate the influence of method dressing on the red carpet’s current proclivity towards extremes. Everyone is trying to capitalize on the sort of hype that the Met Gala ensembles can drum up. Your reference needs to be loud enough to cut through the noise—pyrotechnics optional. It’s perhaps why avant-garde, couture, and couture-level designers like Wun, who dressed a large handful of stars at this year’s Met Gala, are carving out more and more space among these sorts of promotional events. At the same time, brands like Standing Ground and Matieres Fecales—it’s only a matter of time before Fanning wears one of those skirt suits—are also gaining traction as stylists treat the red carpet more like a magazine editorial than an occasion to get dressed up.

Then last night, Grace added another layer. She and Dickson have begun to look outside the films to reference looks of Jennifer Lawrence’s own. Lawrence played Katniss in the first movies. For a Madrid premiere, she wore a Miss Sohee couture dress that paid homage to the Givenchy naked dress Lawrence wore for this year’s Golden Globes. It all feels very meta, calling into question the role of fashion in these promotional spaces, real or fictional. On the IRL Hunger Games red carpet, the reference is dystopian extravagance—but with the rise of method dressing and the corresponding celebration of larger, louder looks, has dystopian extravagance already become the norm? In this case, it may not be life and death, but it could make or break your movie.

#6806. Dakota Johnson Does High-Low Dressing in a Hoodie and Tons of Diamonds

Every product on this page was chosen by a Harper's BAZAAR editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Diamonds—and hoodies—really are a girl’s best friend. On Thursday night, Dakota Johnson offered up a dose of high-low dressing for a date night with Machine Gun Kelly.

Photographed outside of West Hollywood Italian hotspot Dan Tana’s, after attending a party hosted by Khaite, the Fifty Shades of Grey actor was seen in a monochrome little black look with subtle details. Her high-waisted miniskirt was trimmed with scalloped lace, her Gucci leather knee-high boots featured the house’s signature horsebit hardware, and her quilted shoulder bag from Valentino was studded with gold spikes.

To further accessorize, Johnson added six figures’ worth of jewelry, selecting a strand of tiger-eye beads from Laoli, set with four diamonds and an additional diamond pendant, and a 42-carat diamond necklace from Jessica McCormack.

Finally, to dress things down, she added a black zip-up sweatshirt to the ensemble. The cozy piece, with extra-long arms, a boxy silhouette, and a cropped hem, was embroidered with red lettering—identifying it as merch from Snoop Dogg’s production company, Death Row Pictures.

Next to her, MGK was seen wearing a deep-green satin bomber jacket over a yellow graphic hoodie, sweatpants, and fuzzy slippers—taking casual dressing to the next level. The two stars were first spotted together in September, after supposedly meeting at Taylor Swift and Travis Kelce’s July 3 wedding. While they have yet to confirm the relationship publicly, they have been frequently photographed together.

The rumored relationship followed Johnson’s breakup with artist Role Model. The actor, who previously dated Coldplay’s Chris Martin, joked about her tendency to date musicians while hosting Saturday Night Live—teasing that while most fans leave concerts with a T-shirt, she leaves them with long-term relationships.

#6805. This Colorful California Mountain Home Gives Classic Ski House Style a Fresh New Look

Using the surrounding landscape as palette inspiration, designer Alden Fletcher brought 70s retro flair to this family retreat.

When most people think of a classic ski chalet, they picture log-lined walls, dark corners, a raging hearth, plenty of plaid, and maybe even a taxidermied deer or two. But a certain kind of magic strikes when high-altitude architecture hits the right notes, and this Truckee, California, retreat is a masterclass in making the mountains sing.

Built almost 20 years ago, this family holiday home boasted all the 90s mountain classics: an abundance of knotty pine, traditional slate floors, and a minimalist beige palette. Undoubtedly stylish for the time, the retreat now felt out of step with the Bay Area family looking to make it into a personalized winter escape. The owners knew updates were imminent (necessary, even), but they feared losing the home’s classic California soul. Fighting modernism’s white-box aesthetic, they called in Alden Fletcher of the eponymous San Francisco-based design firm to meld the home’s retro architecture with a fresh, forest-inspired brief.

Noting the owner’s concerns and dedication to sustainability, the project’s goal was not to remodel for grandeur, but to restore the home’s charm. Fletcher’s task was to design around storied memories, the family’s obvious love for winter sports, and their passion for entertaining—all while combining the crisp aesthetic of the alpine wilderness with cozy textiles and natural details to create the ultimate mountain sanctuary. By pulling saturated hues from the surrounding landscape and sourcing perky patterns inspired by the local culture, the redesign breathes new life into the slope-side abode without sacrificing sentiment.

FAST FACTSDesigner: Alden Fletcher Interiors Stylist: Beth Protass Location: Truckee, CAThe Space: A five-bedroom, four-bathroom home across 3,000 square feet

The home's hallways set the tone for the entire design scheme. On the first floor, just beyond the main door, wall-mounted ski runners double as decor. Upstairs, Fletcher redefined the boundaries of the primary suite to carve out a nook for a vignette displaying the home’s most bespoke decor piece: a vintage cherry-red chairlift. “The home’s location is unique,” adds Fletcher. “It sits on a ski slope, and you can view chairlifts out the back windows!”

A true gathering space, the living room was designed around the original stone-faced fireplace, though Fletcher did replace the former pine mantel with white oak, connecting the floor details with the exposed oak beams running along the ceiling.

Swiveling burnt orange armchairs accompany an inky indigo sectional. Both gather around a button-tufted ottoman, which doubles as a coffee perch or game table. Shaded sconces flank the fireplace to give the room a glow, even when the hearth is unlit, while a pair of vintage ski shoes hang on the wall, crowning the design.

In the playroom, the deep green hue of the wallpaper paired with the natural texture of knotty pine borrows inspiration from the forest-like landscape just beyond the window. Vertical lines carried through both the wallpaper and the wainscoting create the illusion of height, while playful ski motifs bring the local sport’s culture into the play space.

“The clients are sustainably minded and didn't want to get rid of what was working,” says Fletcher. With conservation in mind, she opted to keep the original perimeter cabinets, refreshing them with paint, new hardware, and Virginia Black Satin Granite countertops, a sturdier alternative to show-stopping soapstone.

To optimize the space, she nixed the two existing islands, reimagining the room to fit a larger iteration that boasts a sink and easily seats five. The design team then added upper cabinets, transforming the space into a storage haven. Later, she placed a library ladder and golden hardware for easy access and a touch of whimsy. Then, she turned her attention to making sure the lighting was up to par. Fletcher firmly believes a beautiful space should be equally as functional. To ensure no detail fell short, she installed discreet recessed lights alongside under-cabinet lighting, and two large glass pendants to adorn the space. All dimmable for ambiance—of course. “There’s nothing worse than a home kitchen that is just flooded with bright light everywhere. A kitchen should still feel cozy and warm, and good lighting is the key to accomplishing that,” says Fletcher.

Fletcher compensated for this former powder room’s lack of windows with vibrant wallpaper to energize the space and make the most of interior lighting. Borrowing space from an adjacent closet, she converted this former two-piece bath into a full retreat with a shower, vanity, and storage, finishing the space with gold hardware for an additional touch of luxury.

Perhaps the most customized room in the home, the mudroom boasts bold color and smart storage solutions for a space as functional as it is chic. The custom cabinet is an ode to smart design, featuring bench seating, pegs, an overhead shelf, and under-seat shoe storage—but the real showstopper is its bold red hue.

“My favorite element of the built-in is the color. The brick color has just the right amount of earthiness,” says Fletcher.

The family loves to entertain, often hosting large groups of friends and family. To accommodate their social lifestyle, Fletcher designed a hospitality hub. Removing a bathroom door, she altered the room’s layout, allowing space for this guest suite to come to life.

With the new dimensions secured, she covered the walls in printed wallpaper inspired by the great local outdoors. The bold pattern set the tone for the rest of the design as she pulled colors, patterns, and themes to complement it.

For the primary bedroom, the theme was warmth: a warm bed, warm home, warm soul, all channeled through muted terracotta walls. But before it could truly transform, the team had to iron out a few oddball details.

“This room used to have a few odd details, including transom windows to the adjoining bathroom and an exterior door to a deck. We removed those elements, which allowed for a better furniture layout and a cozier feeling,” says Fletcher.

The room features a traditional four-poster bed in a nearly black finish, which carries over to the lighting details and furniture hardware, bringing out the warmth and depth of the surrounding rich red hues.

For the primary bath, the team capitalized on the luxury of the sloped-side views, framing a classic 400-pound slipper tub with a nearly floor-to-ceiling window featuring vintage pine trim. Truly the focal point of the design, Fletcher finished the window treatment with woven shades to tie in the natural wood details of the home and stand up to moisture. Natural grain runs through the room’s doors and vanity, topped with soft white Calacatta marble, channeling the classic ski chalet aesthetic. Gold fixtures extend from the vanity to the lighting, towel rack, and tub, for a touch of glamour.

Boasting the project’s biggest splurge, a custom bunk-trundle bed, the kid’s room combines playful hues with forest-inspired details and maximum sleepover potential. “We wanted to maximize sleeping spots in this home,” Fletcher explains. “The Casa Kids bunk bed in the kid's room was a splurge, but it can sleep 5!”

Alden Fletcher first fell in love with design while working as an art teacher and refurbishing furniture in her downtime. In 2020, she grew that passion into a full-service design firm focused on bringing functionality to storied designs and creating ultra-personalized interiors.

#6801. 9 Curb Appeal Trends That Will Take Over in 2027, According to Designers

These are the exterior elements that are getting a major refresh.

Every item on this page was hand-picked by a House Beautiful editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Curb appeal is more than having a pretty house.

“It's not just about how a home looks, but how it lives,” says Danielle Faustini, landscape designer and owner of Helix Green Designs. Homeowners and designers alike are stepping up their game when it comes to creating an appealing facade—in some cases, the shift is quite literal, as stairs are becoming an “it” feature.

The latest U.S. Houzz Outdoor Trends study is seeing a shift toward natural elements like wood cladding, beige exteriors, and native plants.

"Professionals on Houzz have shared that homeowners are looking for exteriors that feel grounded and connected to their surroundings," says Annie Thornton, Houzz outdoor trends expert. “Warm beiges and wood siding blend naturally with stone, native plantings and other organic landscape materials, and they suit a wide range of architectural styles. Our studies show that people are moving away from the stark white and cool gray palettes of recent years toward something that feels more timeless, a look that adds a sense of history without tipping into nostalgia.”

Simple upgrades, like a fresh coat of paint on the front door, are also pivoting into colorways anchored in the past. Designer Alexis Warren is seeing a shift toward darker, moodier front doors in shades of green, deep purple, and near-black, like Farrow & Ball’s Paean Black.

Here are 10 curb-appeal-boosting design trends for the year ahead to make your house the envy of the neighborhood.

Introducing a sculptural element—like an outdoor artwork or creatively placed flora—sends a message that your house has a point of view.

“It elevates the sense of arrival and separates your home from other homes in the vicinity,” says designer Lisa Erdmann. To ensure your home truly stands out, look for one-of-a-kind pieces.

For the home pictured above, Robert J. Bell, founder of landscape architecture firm Bell Design, commissioned artisans to create a copper light inspired by a feather. “More than the end of minimalism, the new focus on sculptural shapes brings back a sense of craft, and the expressive and sensory nature that a simple black metal and glass fixture cannot possess,” he says.

To contrast against the patina, Bell chose purple bougainvillea and a sunny yellow paint on the door.

It’s all about adding warmth and texture, and that’s one of the reasons that beige exterior colors have more than doubled in popularity in the last two years, jumping from 10 to 24 percent, according to Houzz.

Think of a palette that extends into the landscape—warm neutrals such as tan, soft cream, khaki, sand, and greige, says Thornton, who also notes that natural wood and wood-look cladding, sandy stucco, and stone are a welcome change.

Adding paneling can mimic the warmth of wood without worrying about upkeep, points out Francisca Alonso, cofounder and CEO of AV Architects + Builders. This trick also helps soften the front elevation. “We like to combine minimalistic modern details with warm tones to make it more inviting and less commercial-looking,” Alonso adds.

A picket fence may be just what your house needs, but that doesn’t mean you have to resort to the predictable white paint.

“Adding a low-profile picket fence is one of the best ways to gently delineate a property line without in any way compromising sightlines of the home,” says Joelle Nesen, principal designer at Maison Inc. “The picket fence has the kind of classical lines that work well with an historic home like this Craftsman in Portland. By not defaulting to the expected white and leaving the fence a natural wood, the picket fence, with its carefully spaced slats that are almost see-through, allows it to blend in with the landscaping and trees that surround the home.”

When you choose gravel over grass or pavers, it’s all about an understated but classy look.

“Gravel and other permeable aggregates like crushed shells are some of my favorite materials for creating more informal outdoor living spaces,” says Faustini. “It is a beautifully tactile and sensory material. Who doesn't love the soft crunch underfoot? And it really softens a space, helping blur the line between outdoor living areas and the surrounding landscape.”

The look works especially well with decking and patinated light fixtures.

Adding native plants around your house feels cohesive, relaxed, and rooted in place, Thornton says.

Rachael Grochowski, founder and principal of RHG Architecture + Design, adds that she expects to see an increase in native landscapes, meadow-type gardens, as well as an integration of clover into lawns.

“The trend to embrace natural landscapes is not only about supporting environment regeneration, but it also supports our wellbeing by instigating wonder, beauty, and randomness,” she says.

Curb appeal is all about first impressions, and homeowners are not taking this feature lightly. Houzz reports that searches for “front walkway” have more than quadrupled (up 357 percent) in the first three quarters of 2026, compared with the same period last year.

Step design, in particular, is one feature that is growing in popularity.

When Sarah Crawford, associate designer with Courtney B. Smith Design, renovated her home, she added steps to her 1920s bungalow to literally and figuratively elevate the experience. The home initially had just one step from the sidewalk to the front door. By adding a series of stairs to gently guide the way to the door, she added a quiet sense of elegance in keeping with the wood-shingled exterior. Key to the transformation was selecting materials that reflect the character and style of the original home, in this case reclaimed granite. Scale and balance were essential to adding a series of stairs to gently guide visitors to the door.

“Steps should never be narrower than the doorway,” Crawford says. “In fact, the wider the steps, the more inviting the approach.”

For a different approach to the front entrance, consider stone pathways, especially those with a casual look, like grass pavers.

Interior designer Darci Hether likes large-format bluestone pavers, which offer a clean, timeless look. “The generous scale of the stones creates a sense of openness, while the greenery between them softens the overall appearance and adds a beautiful, natural element,” she explains.

Erdmann encourages her clients to forgo safe neutrals and treat the front entry door as a place for personality.

“Whether it is hot pink, lipstick red, cobalt blue, or Benjamin Moore’s color of the year, Webster Green—make a statement!” she says. “Don’t match the door to the shutters and choose something fun. A front door is the easiest, lowest-risk place to experiment since it takes one afternoon and a quart of paint.”

Case in point, Elizabeth Cross-Beard, interior designer behind Palm Henri, took to a vibrant peacock blue to accentuate the lines of the above 1925 French manor house. “When paired with natural materials and muted tones, [the paint] brings just enough color to intrigue the eye without disrupting the overall sense of classic grandeur. Unlike other blue tones which can call too much attention to them, this rich color is the front-door sweet spot,” Cross-Beard explains.

Go beyond ho-hum pots that stand sentry at your door. Look for large pieces with personality to anchor the entrance.

“Aged terracotta, weathered stone, or antique urns bring patina and character, helping even a newly built home feel established,” says MK Smith Boykin of Smith Interiors. “With that kind of presence, the planting itself can be quite simple.” The details are especially important when your home has architectural symmetry, like columns.

“Using matching plants only increases the allure of perfect visual balance,” Nesen adds. “The key is that the scale of the planters needs to match the proportions of the door and porch: Selecting pots or containers that are too small will immediately drain the drama and beauty of the front door statement.”

#6799. Rosie Huntington-Whiteley Goes Completely Backless in a Sculptural Gucci Dress

She made a statement at the 2026 LACMA Gala

At the 2026 LACMA Art+Film Gala, Rosie Huntington-Whiteley was all business in the front, party in the back. Showing up to the Los Angeles County Museum of Art’s annual black-tie event—where everyone from philanthropists to actors to musicians to athletes came together for a glamorous evening of fundraising—the model was seen wearing a sleek black dress with a surprise.

Courtesy of Gucci, the evening’s sponsor, the piece appeared simple and modest at first glance—with a front defined by a sculptural, exaggerated high neckline and a slim-fit long-sleeve, floor-length silhouette. When Huntington-Whiteley turned, however, the dress was revealed to be (almost) entirely open, with a singular piece of silver hardware creating separation between a low scoop back and a high leg slit.

And, if that wasn’t enough, the whole garment was lined with dark-green sequins, which shimmered subtly in the light. She matched the shade to her diamond-and emerald earrings, which were on full display thanks to her slicked-back ponytail. She slipped into pointed-toe black pumps and stopped for photos in front of Chris Burden’s famed public art installation, Urban Light, which doubled as a red carpet for the evening.

The Art+Film Gala celebrated its 15th year tonight, supporting the museum’s ongoing efforts to expand its film programming. It honored the work of painter Vija Celmins, known for her photo-realistic work exploring the complexity of nature, as well as Dune filmmaker Denis Villeneuve. Blackpink’s Rosé was tapped for a musical performance, and, like every year, Leonardo DiCaprio and LACMA trustee Eva Chow served as co-chairs.

#6798. Is Your Home Too Matchy-Matchy? These 5 Designer Tricks Break Up the Blah and Add Instant Character

If your bedroom design began with a furniture set that matches your nightstands and lamps, it may be time to shake things up. Having a space where everything coordinates perfectly may seem like the easiest route to a stylish home, but many times, it can feel more sterile than a true reflection of who you are and what you love.

“A home often looks sterile when everything comes from the same set,” explains Preeti Shah, founder and principal designer at Spotlight Style.

By stepping out of your comfort zone and switching things up a bit, you can create a cohesive look that still brings character and depth to your home. Here are five designer tips to steer you in the right direction.

Just because one of your surfaces is natural white oak doesn't mean everything in the same room needs to follow suit.

“We love mixing dark wood tones with light tones and everything in between,” says Leah Hook, founder and principal designer at Gray Oak Studio.

Repeating wood tones can make a room feel cohesive, but you don't need to use the same tone everywhere. Think outside the box and look for subtle ways to tie the space together, such as repeating a wood tone in an art frame or decorative tray.

Believe it or not, your island does not have to match your kitchen countertops in every possible way.

“Choose a more exciting stone for your island. You can even miter it two inches thick instead of keeping everything the standard 1.25-inch thickness,” explains Gaelle Dudley, founder and creative director at GLDESIGN.

Dining chairs are almost always purchased as a matching set, but sourcing different styles from flea markets or Facebook Marketplace can give your dining room a fun, unexpected look.

“Select just one element from the table that will be repeated in the chairs, and go with something from a different collection or even an entirely different furniture line,” says Pattie Kelly, certified interior decorator and owner of Inspired Home Interiors.

Consider the height of the chairs (so that they still make sense together at the table) and how much room you have to pull them out in (so you’re not running into the wall behind them).

The most interesting homes display a mix of different art styles. Instead of sticking to one color palette or aesthetic, seek out pieces that contrast. For instance, Shah is a fan of contrasting graphic black-and-white drawings with gold-veined abstract canvases in various rooms of the home.

Speaking of artwork, as the space pictured above by Nina Garbiras illustrates, don’t be afraid to mix and match your frame materials. Just keep one element consistent, like the mat size or the frame width, so that it still has a curated (not chaotic) feel.

Just because you’re choosing one type of fixture finish doesn’t mean you have to repeat it everywhere. In fact, your space will look more “expensive” if you match shiny finishes very minimally.

For example, the above space by Vestige Home showcases how well brass and matte-black hardware can play off each other, adding depth, harmony, and visual interest. Choosing to go with one finish throughout this space would’ve become too matchy-matchy very quickly.

#6797. The Official Guide To Last-Minute Friendsgiving Appetizers, Based On How Much Time You Have

Consider us your official party-planning committee.

The holiday season is one of the busiest times of the year, so it’s no surprise that Friendsgiving can sneak up on you. If you put your name down to bring an appetizer and put off the planning until crunch time, don’t worry, last-minute appetizers are kind of our thing. Whether you’ve got days to plan ahead or minutes to whip together a party-worthy app, we’ve got you covered with these 10 Friendsgiving appetizers to bring to the party, based on how much time you have. Plus, these apps are so impressive, nobody will ever realize you almost showed up empty-handed.

Looking for more Friendsgiving recipes? Check out the rest of our last-minute Friendsgiving recipes and our top-rated Friendsgiving recipes.

You Want To Get A Jump Start The Night Before: Turkey Cheese Ball

This cheese ball is not only the most eye-catching appetizer you can add to your holiday menu, but it’s surprisingly easy to create. You’ll just have let it chill for about 3 hours in the refrigerator.

You Woke Up Early & Have Plenty Of Time: Sweet Potato Rolls

These rolls use sweet potatoes to make them even softer and fluffier, similar to our classic potato rolls. The tops are brushed with a honey butter and sprinkled with salt to play up both the savory and sweet side of a sweet potato.

You Still Have A Couple Hours To Spare: Cranberry Walnut Cheese Log

Salty, crunchy, and sometimes sweet, this pre-dinner treat is easy and infinitely adaptable for any season and occasion. Here, dried sweetened cranberries, walnuts, and fresh thyme conjure a holiday feeling, while a little honey complements the sweet-tart fruit.

You’re Still In Your Comfy Clothes: Green Bean Casserole Bites

Baked in crescent roll cups with crispy fried onions, a rich, creamy mushroom sauce, and tender-yet-snappy green beans, these handheld wonders hit every note. Have them as an appetizer or even on your plate as a biscuit substitute!

You’re Feeling Good With An Hour To Go: Sausage Stuffing Bites With Cranberry Sauce

Baked stuffing, inside the turkey or out, is a Thanksgiving staple for many families, although the particulars of the go-to recipe can vary. The only thing better than any of these stuffing recipes? MORE. STUFFING. Enter a bite-sized, stuffing-centered appetizer with a tangy, three-ingredient dipping sauce.

You’re Starting To Keep An Eye On The Clock: Brie & Green Bean Bundles

Perfect for a Thanksgiving potluck, these bundles of joy turn the classic green bean casserole into a handheld delight, all wrapped in flaky crescent dough. Wherever you choose to serve them, we guarantee one thing—these flaky and cheesy apps will go fast.

You Just Got A Last-Minute Text From The Host, Asking For Another App: Pumpkin Spice Hot Honey Brie Bites

Sweet and savory with a kick of heat, these pumpkin, Brie, and hot honey bites are the perfect fall appetizer. The honey provides the perfect balance for the sweetness of the pumpkin and the savory, melty Brie. Add a bit of fresh thyme and some roasted pecans, and you’ve got yourself a certified crowd-pleaser.

You’ve Got 30 Minutes To Make Some Magic Happen: Turkuterie

This Friendsgiving, keep your kitchen free of filching fingers by creating the most adorable charcuterie—err, turkuterie—board your friends and family will have ever seen. We’ve packed this cheese board with fruits and cheeses appropriate for an autumnal snack that everyone will want to gobble up!

You’ve Got One Shoe On Already: Cranberry Whipped Feta Dip

A holiday cheeseboard is great and all, but why go to all the trouble when you can make this sweet, salty, creamy, and tart dip with a fraction of the time and effort Fresh feta cheese is combined with herbs, garlic, and cream cheese, whipped until super-smooth, then topped with tart homemade cranberry sauce, toasted pistachios, and honey.

Oh No, Weren’t You Supposed To Bring Something?!: Antipasto Bites

When we need a last-minute appetizer for any party, we always turn to these antipasto skewers. They're ready in just 15 minutes, are perfectly shareable, and have all of the varied flavors and textures of an antipasto spread.

#6796. Nicole Kidman Celebrates the Art of Ballet in a Voluminous Chanel Tutu

She made her LACMA Gala debut in a messy high ponytail

Every year, the Los Angeles County Museum of Art welcomes the art, film, fashion, and entertainment industries to its annual fundraising event. Since its inception back in 2011, the Art+Film Gala, as it is known, has become a staple fixture on the fall calendar, seeing everyone from models to actors to philanthropists to musicians to athletes return time and time again. But this year, the starry festivities also saw a new face or two—including the one and only Nicole Kidman.

Making her LACMA debut, Kidman showed up to the event wearing a voluminous Chanel dress from Matthieu Blazy’s Spring/Summer 2027 collection. On top, the taupe-and-cream piece featured a long-sleeve corset bodice, with a raw-edge neckline that contrasted the structured boning below. From the hips, layers and layers of semi-sheer tulle cut at different lengths created a high-low tutu skirt that, even when still, gave the illusion of movement.

Before heading inside, the Babygirl actor—with a messy high ponytail and open-toe heels—stopped for photos by Chris Burden’s Urban Light art installation, which doubled as a red carpet for the evening. She was joined by the likes of Alex Consani, Kim Kardashian, and Paris Hilton.

LACMA’s annual gala was founded to support the museum’s efforts to expand its programming, making film more central to its exhibitions and education. It has placed a focus on the intersection of art and film, and takes a moment each year to celebrate individuals for their contributions to the visual and cinematic arts. Tonight, it honored the work of painter Vija Celmins, known for her photo-realistic explorations of nature, as well as Dune filmmaker Denis Villeneuve. Blackpink’s Rosé was tapped for a musical performance, and, for the 15th year, Leonardo DiCaprio and LACMA trustee Eva Chow served as co-chairs.

#6795. Here’s What It Takes to Keep a Pool Running During the Winter, According to Experts

Before you dive in, it’s important to understand the risks and expenses involved.

When most people think of backyard swimming pools, relaxing outdoors in the summertime immediately comes to mind. But some homeowners don’t want the swimming season to end when temperatures drop. Fans of cold-water swimming are starting to consider ways to enjoy their pools throughout the winter as a part of an at-home wellness experience.

“Ten years ago, most people didn’t think winter swims were possible, especially in colder climates,” says Karen Larson, cofounder of Soake Pools. “Times have changed because these days, they’re seeing neighbors, friends, and others do it and post the proof on social media.”

The increasing interest in cold-water experiences is part of a broader wellness trend. In American College of Sports Medicine's 2026 Worldwide Fitness Trends report, cold therapies, including cold plunges, cryotherapy, and cold showers, earned the 17th spot among fitness trends for the year, up from number 20 in its 2025 report.

If you’ve previously closed your swimming pool around Labor Day but are interested in making cold-water swimming a part of your daily wellness routine, there’s a lot to keep in mind. Not every existing pool is a good candidate for keeping open for winter swimming, and it’s important to understand the risks and expenses involved. But if you’re curious, here’s what to consider about keeping a pool open year-round.

Why is cold-water swimming becoming more common? There are numerous purported mental and wellness benefits, from athletic recovery to stress relief and better sleep. A 2025 systematic review and meta-analysis published in PLOS ONE found that cold-water immersion led to a reduction in stress for 12 hours afterward as well as improvements in sleep quality and overall quality of life.

“Winter swimming often creates a time where friends and family can come together without distractions,” adds Larson.

That said, while swimming during the colder months has its perks, there are also several risks to keep in mind. A 2020 review in the International Journal of Environmental Research and Public Health found that cold-water immersion can trigger an immediate shock response, leading to involuntary gasping, hyperventilation, and a rapid increase in heart rate and blood pressure. These reactions can make it difficult to control your breathing and increase your risk of drowning. Cold temps can also impair your muscle strength and nerve function, making it challenging to swim safely.

It’s a good idea to acclimate to the cold water gradually and avoid prolonged exposure. You’ll also want to make sure there’s someone else home while you take your cold-water plunge in the pool.

In addition, if you have underlying conditions like heart disease, consult your doctor before all else, as the cold water may place additional strain on your heart.

If you’re trying to consider whether your pool is a good candidate to keep open during the colder months, these are the things to look into.

The cost of keeping a swimming pool open for winter swimming will depend on your location, size, desired water temperature, and the type of heater you use.

“Routine pool maintenance generally remains about the same year-round, although chemical consumption may decrease depending on how frequently the pool is used,” explains Jim Eisch, Jr., vice president of operations at Pinch A Penny Pool Patio Spa. “The biggest expense to consider in the winter is heating the pool.”

For this reason, prospective winter swimmers may want to consider an upgrade to their heating system. Instead of using pricey natural gas heating or a traditional heat pump, Eisch says that inverter technology is one of the most affordable ways to maintain a swimmable pool temperature year-round.

For example, if you live in Florida and have a 10,000-gallon swimming pool, Eisch estimates your annual heating costs will be $2,467 with a natural gas heater, $741 with a traditional heat pump, and $434 for a heat/cool pump that uses inverter technology.

As aforementioned, the goal with wintertime swimming is not to be soaking in bathwater-like temperatures while it’s freezing outside. For safety purposes, winter swims are quick, and the heating system is to keep your chlorinated pool water well above the 19-degree Fahrenheit freezing point.

Unsurprisingly, a standard 14-by-28-foot swimming pool is going to be one of the hardest to use effectively year-round, unless you live in a region with a mild wintertime climate.

“You can technically use a large pool year-round, but it’s difficult to cover all its surface area well enough to hold in the heat, so heating bills can climb fast,” says Larson.

Therefore, smaller sizes like a 10-by-16-foot cocktail pool or plunge pool are better candidates for keeping open. Dedicated winter swimmers should also invest in an easy on-and-off cover—or a motorized auto cover—for added protection.

Regular pool cleaning and maintenance are essential, and this upkeep over the winter is no exception.

“Check the filtration system regularly to make sure it’s circulating smoothly, monitor the water chemistry, and plan for power outages—because there’s a good chance they will happen,” Larson explains. For this reason, you should also invest in a generator that can keep the pump running if the power goes out.

Safe pool access is just as important as any other aspect of the pool’s design. That’s why a nonslip surface, excellent drainage, and ample lighting are key.

“Your design should also provide a clear path from the house to the water,” says Neha Gupta, interior designer and founder at NU Curations.

Especially in the wintertime when ice and snow are a factor in your region, make sure you have a safe and smooth exit plan for getting out of the water, bundled up, and into your warm home without any obstacles. And then, of course, remember to cover the pool back up to keep it protected and ready for your next plunge.

#6792. 10 Stylish Sofa Covers to Totally Refresh Your Living Room Before the Holidays

Every item on this page was hand-picked by a House Beautiful editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Hide stains, protect upholstery, and add instant polish.

Life at home gets messy with its regular residents, and it gets even crazier when guests come over. Hosting season demands the most of our couches, as they become extra seats for family dinner or makeshift beds for overnight stays. It’s not always feasible to swap out your sofa just because the upholstery has seen better days. That’s where the mighty sofa cover comes in.

Whether you opt for a tightly-fitted silhouette or a looser, blanket-style slipcover, a high-quality sofa cover can totally refresh your space. Just think about it: a dated, yellowing couch suddenly looks fresh and brand new. You can even go bold and experiment with patterned options, without the commitment of buying a whole new piece. Pro tip: patterns are also a great way to hide stains.

There are hundreds of sofa cover options available, so below, our shopping team did the hard part for you. We hunted down nine options for different types of couches, including an editor-tested and approved option. Plus, we chatted with interior designer Tiffany Leigh for tips on styling sofa covers, keeping them clean, and more.

Velvet couches are having a moment, so if you want in on the trend but don't want to toss out your current sofa, enter this sumptuous sofa slipcover. Stretch the base piece to fit around a sofa measuring 72 to 96 inches, then cover three cushions with the included covers. The fabric is extra soft, so you feel like you're sitting on a high-end sofa at a fraction of the price. "My family used this velvet slipcover to bring a dated, faded blue sofa back to life in a trendy ivory velvet that looked like it could be at home in any furniture showroom," Associate Shopping Editor Janae McKenzie says. "Even better: it was so soft against my skin that I fell asleep on it during movie night more times than I'm proud of."

This blanket-style cover drapes easily over a two-three cushion couch, and it’s made of a non-slip fabric designed to stay in place. Even with the rowdiest kids and pets.

Just because you want to protect your couch doesn’t mean you have to give up dreams of elegant white furniture. This extra-large cream couch cover actually comes in two pieces, so it’s perfect to cover an L-Shaped or U-Shaped sectional. You can protect the chaise arm with ease, and it’s still stain-resistant and machine-washable.

Whether you're worried about pet or kid accidents, or an errant wine spill during book club, this waterproof cover could be a great solution to protect your beloved furniture. The stretchy jacquard fabric is leakproof and fits snug to your cushions and frame.

The fitted fabric makes it appear as though you bought the sofa in this style, but you can easily swap it out when you want a different look or feel. It's all in one piece for your sofa's frame, so you can still add the throw pillows of your choice.

Material: Basketweave, Linen, Boucle, Velvet, and more | Colors: 95 | Care: Depends on the fabric material you chose

The size of your sofa cover will depend not only on the size of the sofa or couch you want to protect, but also on the properties of the fabric itself. Leigh says that with a stretchy fabric, you may want a cover with measurements slightly smaller than the dimensions of your sofa for a tighter, more fitted look. For sofa covers with corner ties that allow you to tighten or tie bows, the fabric may have a bit less give, so you'll want to allow for a little extra room in the cover size.

Some styles lend themselves to being covered more effectively than others. Leigh says that sofas with thinner or rolled arms tend to look nicer with a slipcover than those without. You can also choose between having a skirt on your sofa cover or showing off the legs of your furniture, depending on your aesthetic preference. “I like when they go right to the floor because a skirt cover is a much more traditional, covered look,” Leigh says. “If you have a little bit more of a modern style, having a slipcover that stops at the legs can be a fresher, kind of more updated take.”

Natural fabrics, such as chenille or linen, are typically a great choice when selecting a sofa cover fabric. For family rooms or homes with pets, indoor/outdoor fabrics like Crypton that have been treated to repel dirt and debris are safer bets for those wanting something stain-resistant. The stretchiness of the fabric also plays a role in selecting a sofa cover. "If you wanted something a little bit more durable, canvas or cotton would be a really great material on there," Leigh says.

It's always a good idea to pay attention to the care instructions for whatever cover you buy, as fabric care really depends on the individual material. For example, a heavier cotton material can likely withstand more washing. “I would say only wash when you really need to, just like with a really nice sweater,” Leigh says. “Don't wash it every time you wear it because washing breaks down fibers. So, it could reduce the longevity of your sofa cover. Most are designed to be removed and washed. So if and when you feel like 'oh, you know what, it's looking a little bit dingy, it's time,' that's the time to do it.” Additionally, it's essential to consider the size of your cover, as larger ones may necessitate the use of a commercial washing machine.

Additionally, the cover may wrinkle over time, especially with linen, which softens with each wash. “Linen does get a little bit wrinkly, but I think that's kind of the charm of a slipcovered sofa, having that kind of rumpled, you know, 'someone just took a nap here' look,” Leigh says.

“An oatmeal linen slipcover is that very quintessential, relaxed coastal style that you see a lot of slipcovers used in, and I think it's a classic choice that you'll never go wrong with,” Leigh says. Don't run away from patterns either, though—Leigh is seeing a rise in patterned upholstery in the design industry, and it's a chance to push the envelope and have fun with your design choices. “A cover can be a great way to, you know, try out a pattern without necessarily committing to it forever, because if in five years you say, ‘okay, this pattern was fun, but I'm ready for something different,’ you can just change the slipcover as opposed to having to get an all-new sofa,” Leigh says.

At House Beautiful, we are experts in reviewing and buying anything for the home, from mattresses for back pain to reclining sofas. For this story in particular, we asked our staff members to share their favorite sofa cover (if they own one) and spent hours combing the internet, reading hundreds of reviews about the best ones currently available on the market. While we haven't personally tested every product on this list, we've thoroughly outlined the pros and cons of each sofa cover to provide you with all the necessary details before you make a purchase.

For further insight into how to best place sofa covers in your home, we consulted interior designer Tiffany Leigh. Leigh runs her eponymous firm in Ontario, Canada, where her team helps clients bring their dream homes and cottages to life. Since 2019, they have designed approachable and livable interiors that feel welcoming and elevated, focusing on quality craftsmanship, emotional connection, and natural elements.
