#6753. 18 Innovative Hair Products ELLE Editors Chose for 2026

From a split-end repair balm to a tangle-free hairbrush, these are our Future of Beauty winners.

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Should you try an overnight mask? Or a serum? What about a repair balm? And no, not for your face—for your hair. Though products like these used to be exclusively reserved for skin care routines, in recent years, our hair has gotten the multistep treatment. But with so many new products on the market, it can be difficult to figure out which are worth buying and which are better to skip. For the 2026 Future of Beauty Awards, ELLE testers sprayed their hair with leave-ins, massaged scrubs into their scalps, and styled with various gels and creams to find 18 game-changing hair products. Read more below.

If you want curl definition without sacrificing movement, this medium-hold gel is made for you. Our testers found that a thin layer of this lightweight jelly encouraged curls to clump together beautifully and tamed frizz for hours, all while leaving their hair soft, shiny, humidity-resistant, and touchable. Even better, the results held up beyond wash day. “This gel keeps my curls defined from first-to-second-day hair,” our tester shared, adding, “Usually it’s a mess by day two.”

SH-RD Beauty Corp. Exosome Root Awakening Concentrate

With 50 billion exosomes in a bottle, this professional-grade scalp serum is a huge boon for hair loss. Our tester says that she would buy this again. “I chopped off three inches a month ago and there seems to be about half an inch of growth already,” she said. One pro tip: Pair it with a DIY scalp massage to make sure it distributes evenly throughout your roots.

Kérastase Chronologiste Overnight Youth Serum

Shampooing and conditioning can only do so much to reverse damage—the real work happens post-shower. This serum works while you sleep, hydrating and fortifying strands while boosting shine. Our tester, who is very familiar with repair products, noticed that “it made my hair softer and appear less damaged” and declared it “more effective than others I have tried.”

Nexxus Keraphix Liquid Crystallizer Hair Strengthening Treatment

“Liquid Crystallizer” isn’t just a cool name—this product is made with liquid crystal amino acids, which work to rebuild broken bonds in your cuticle and strengthen it. And our tester can verify that it really works. “After the first use on damp hair, it felt refreshed and healthier, like I had been to a salon. The strands also felt plumper—back to my pre-bleach days. It still felt clean, unlike with some leave-ins, even when used dry. Several showers and uses later, I’m loving it.”

Olaplex N°.3Plus Hair Repair Pre-Shampoo Treatment

“I was hooked from the moment I first used this product,” our tester raved. “It immediately makes my hair buttery and hydrated as soon as I smooth it on. It’s easy to use and on top of that, it smells great and rinses out well. It’s also lightweight and can be applied generously without feeling oversaturated. It feels like exactly what my hair needs and something I could get addicted to using.” Apply it before you shampoo to repair damage and split ends thanks to the brand’s signature bond-building technology, which helps rebuild damaged hair from the inside out.

If you’re in the market for a slick-back aid that doesn’t leave a greasy film (or require multiple showers to get out), this stick is what you’ve been looking for. “I was really impressed with how little residue it left and the immediate result,” our tester said. She used it on a bun during a humid day and felt that it added shine, nixed flyaways, and kept her hair in place. It contains shea and mkani butters to seal in moisture, while cranberry extract and oil help smooth and add shine.

Don’t let the word “balm” trip you up—this is an in-shower mask that you leave in for three minutes after shampooing, in lieu of conditioner. Our tester worried it could be greasy, but she was pleasantly surprised: “My hair was quickly silky, shiny, and less frizzy.” She thought it worked better than her typical conditioner, with a bonus that it “smells amazing.”

Our tester said it best: “Did I know there were metals in my hair? No. But apparently there are microplastics in our brains so I guess it shouldn’t surprise me. I’ve been using this shampoo twice a week and my hair feels very clean and free of build-up. I love the scent, too. Even my hair dresser remarked on how shiny my hair is right now.”

Imagine a product that works so well coworkers start to comment on how good your hair looks lately. That’s what happened to our tester when she started adding this to her routine: “My hair appears healthier and softer, with people at work even asking what I’ve been using. My ends are a lot less dry.” It makes sense, since the acidic bonding complex works to seal split ends in your hair.

“I’ll admit I was skeptical that a salt scrub would actually serve as a shampoo replacement, but I am a convert now!” said our tester. “Under the salt layer, there’s a thick cream that works into a robust lather, effectively serving as a shampoo. The scent, inspired by Big Sur, is green and fresh without being overpowering.”

This prep product provides heat protection up to 410 degrees Fahrenheit and smooths and fends off frizz using amla oil and plant actives. Our tester said, “The product immediately made my hair feel super pliable and hydrated without any gloopy feel or residue. This held true after the blow-dry and iron too—I am super happy with the soft feel and shiny texture after using this product. It lasted several days without any frizz or greasiness.”

Dr. Groot Scalp Barrier Boost Oil in Serum Leave-In Hair and Scalp Treatment

As with your skin care routine, this serum nourishes your scalp with proven ingredients like hyaluronic acid and ferulic acid, rosemary extracts, biotin, and vitamins A, C, and E. Our tester loved how lightweight this oil felt and noticed that it really helped reduce the thinning around her temples.

Sisley Paris Revitalizing Fortifying Super Serum Advanced Formula

This serum helps hair look denser and restrains grays, thanks to a nourishing blend of rice proteins, yeast, and einkorn wheat, plus a potent mix of vitamins. Serums often feel drippy or sticky, but our tester noted this one had a silky, soothing feel that quickly soothed her scalp, as well as her hair anxiety.

The very same gel that slicks Beyoncé’s hair can also be yours. Polymers, plant lipids, and black honey fermentation protect the hair up to 450 degrees Fahrenheit, while the brand’s signature blend of desert plant oils moisturizes. Our tester said, “As someone who sports a slickback weekly, I loved this product! It is not sticky, but has a very strong hold. I did not experience any flaky residue that hard gel can sometimes leave behind. My slickback felt smooth and sleek, instead of hard and crunchy.”

La Bonne Brosse Freshening and Volumizing Hair Mist

Unlike a chalky dry shampoo, this scented mist uses rice water and niacinamide to purify the roots, with no residue. Our tester liked using this as a post-gym product. “It’s not a dry shampoo, but my hair definitely felt fresher after using this. While it didn’t fully absorb oil, it did give it a quick, non-crunchy refresh.”

“I am in desperate need of a cut, but with this, you wouldn’t even be able to tell!” raved our tester. The gloss adds luminosity through the brand’s bioidentical keratin. “I liked the watery texture of this formula because it added moisture without weight.”

Already known as an expert brand for gently detangling wet hair, Tangle Teezer’s new brush also adds shine and smooths. The bristles are shaped like mini, pointy cones and placed on a diagonal to minimize damage and gently brush through snarls. Our tester said, “I have a very fragile scalp and other brushes often feel too harsh. This brush was so gentle and left me with ultrashiny hair.”

Our tester said that this innovative “sealing water” already feels like a part of her “forever routine.” It hydrates, adds shine, and protects from heat damage with hyaluronic acid and white orchid extract. “I’m not typically someone who uses leave-in conditioners because I always worry they will leave my hair feeling greasy or weighed down,” she shared. “Not this one—it’s super light, smells amazing, and makes my hair feel protected without weighing it down or greasing it up. It has also never looked shinier.”

#6751. Artist Quentin Jones Launches a Surrealist Jewelry Collection

With Nocturne, the House of Quentin Jones founder brings the playful, sculptural language of her homewares to a collection of wearable art.

Above: Artist Quentin Jones wears pieces from her new jewelry collection while posing in front of tapestries Circe’s Garden and Western Moon.

British mixed-media artist Quentin Jones has quite the varied resume—one that spans artistic disciplines and collaborators as varied as Chanel, Apple, and Miley Cyrus. Now based in Brooklyn, her practice blends filmmaking, photography, collage, and painting.

Last year, Jones turned her eye towards interiors, launching a homeware line and design studio called House of Quentin Jones. The brand debuted with a collection of tapestries and ceramic sculptures that are both architectural and playful, leaning into Jones’s moody, dreamy look. Now, after a year of HOQJ, Jones is releasing new styles—and adding jewelry to the mix.

Nocturne, a collection of sculptural jewelry, translates Jones’s eclectic design language into wearable art. She pulled motifs like eyes, hands, and celestial forms from her tapestries and ceramics and incorporated them into the pieces. The collection features earrings, pendants, chains, and cuffs that balance black and white, pearl and onyx, organic and sculptural shapes.

“I’m interested in jewelry as sculpture,” Jones tells ELLE Decor. “A cuff wraps around an arm, a collar frames the neck, a tassel moves. The body becomes part of the composition. I want the pieces to have a presence of their own, while leaving room for the personality of the person wearing them.”

As she enters this new category, Jones is also beefing up her existing offerings. She’s adding new quilted tapestries to the studio’s repertoire and iterating on previous designs. With these new pieces, she says, “The palette has become softer and earthier—ivory, sand, and burgundy—but the compositions still have that surreal, graphic quality that runs through my work.”

But she isn’t stopping there. Jones has big dreams for the future of her eponymous studio, a “House” built larger with each new release. Next up, she’d like to expand the accessories category further, hoping to add bags, scarves, and other wearable pieces to her lineup.

“I’ve spent years working between art, film, and fashion,” she says. “I’m building towards a collection of things that can accompany you through your life—from what hangs on your wall to what you choose to wear.”

#6750. A Kitchen in Bloom: Inside Miele’s Lush Botanical Oasis Where Art Meets Design

Step inside Art Basel as visionaries Tiffany Howell and Asher Young merge cutting-edge technology with untamed botanical romance, staging an immersive sensory landscape you don’t just see, but feel.

Miele crafts appliances for enduring performance, but they also curate experiences that engage all the senses. Imagine the intuitive precision of a sleek induction cooktop that subtly adjusts temperature on its own; the soft, inviting glow of a French door refrigerator that reveals a curated bounty within ample space; the alluring aroma of a built-in coffee system greeting you each morning; and the calming, almost meditative hum of a steam at work. Each of these choreographed moments elevate Miele appliances beyond a functional tool into something you fundamentally feel. By blending groundbreaking innovation and sustainable features with timeless, minimalist design, Miele frees you to embrace the pure joy of cooking and to savor the intimate, everyday moments that make a house a true home.

For this year’s Art Basel, the ultimate intersection of culture and creativity, Miele brings together two visionaries to redefine the culinary space. Tiffany Howell of Night Palm Design is passionate about creating cinematic homes that tell a deeply personal story. Asher Young is a multidisciplinary artist known for merging art with technology to create immersive spectacle. Together, they have imagined a sensory kitchen built entirely within a lush botanical garden setting. Here, the wildness of the natural world frames the precise German engineering. It is a space where the smooth, handleless silhouettes and cool touch of Miele's Obsidian Black glass stand in striking relief against their organic surroundings, harnessing technology for a design that is both tactile and state-of-the-art. “We used the sleek, matte black Miele appliances as the driving force to bring this to life,” says Howell of the striking visual contrast between nature and cutting-edge machines. “They’re like the perfect little black dress—timeless, elegant, and grounding the entire room.” A deep respect for the environment is also reflected in Miele engineering, which uses innovative technology that preserves ingredients longer—ensuring that the natural world isn't just an aesthetic inspiration, but a core priority for the brand.

Howell’s signature nostalgic romance blends with Asher’s analytical thinking and knack for creating physical, interactive experiences to make the space come alive. Young's touch ensures the installation feels genuinely dynamic, translating the unseen power of Miele’s technology into a captivating physical experience. “How can we create a space that rebalances and also is a place that you can actually engage with? So you can smell the flowers while you're washing your hand. You can feel the cool breeze as you open the fridge,” explains Young. “All these elements is not just for the sake of a visual presentation, but actually to experience and be inside.” The result is an experiential reminder that kitchens should always reflect your personality and passions. “We both said let’s bring our shared language together,” he recalls. The finished design is the undeniable heartbeat of the garden, a space with one foot rooted elegantly in the past and the other stepping boldly into the future.

“A kitchen expresses how you live, what you love and desire, and it’s also where your most cherished memories are made,” says Howell. As the Miami sun sets, the botanical space seamlessly transitions into an elegant, moody enclave for evening entertaining, inviting you to linger just a little bit longer. And in a Miele kitchen, those memories continue into the next day, beginning the moment the fragrant aroma of freshly brewed coffee announces the limitless possibilities of a brand-new morning.

Experience the convergence of culinary art, design, and innovation firsthand. Explore the full Miele collection and begin designing a kitchen that awakens every sense.

#6748. ELLE Editors Share the Best Color Cosmetics and Nail Products of 2026

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

We’re calling it: 2026 was the year of makeup. After many years of clean girl beauty and an emphasis on minimalism, it’s time to explore colors and self-expression again. This year’s best products go far beyond the typical mascara or run-of-the-mill concealer. Our testers raved about a brow pencil that relies on friction to amp up volume, blushes that looked natural and stayed put for hours, and lip stains that mimicked popsicle-like shades. For all of this and more, keep reading below for our 2026 Future of Beauty winners. We found 26 (the most ever!) color cosmetics worth your attention, spanning lips, eyes, face, and even nails.

For anyone looking to extend the life of their manicure without a trip to the salon, these ultra-thin tips strike an impressive balance between durability and a natural-looking length and finish. Our tester said they were easy to shape and blend seamlessly: “I especially love how thin they look and feel. They give you a little extra length and structure without feeling bulky.” Better yet, the set lasted for weeks and was easy to apply and remove at home.

Charlotte Tilbury Airbrush Flawless Long-Wear Blur Concealer

The creamy formula brightens, blurs, and smooths with a soft-matte finish, proving that full coverage doesn’t have to feel heavy. Infused with hyaluronic acid for hydration, the lightweight texture layers seamlessly without settling. Our tester proclaimed it better than another well-known concealer. “Very easy to use, a lot of product, great coverage, and long wear, too.”

Downsize your makeup touch-up bag with this dual-sided product that allows you to precisely line, fill, and blur your lips in one go. “In the age of lip combos, this makes it super easy without carting around multiple items,” our tester said.

If the falsie look is what you’re after, this tubing primer coats lashes with peptides so that they become stronger even before you apply mascara. Our tester loved how this primer made her lashes look too: It separated them prettily and she called it “an insurance policy against clumping.”

Guerlain Meteorites Compact Mattifying Pressed Setting Powder

This mosaic of pressed powders boasts an eight-hour wear time, blurring power, and color-correcting and setting capabilities too. Our tester liked this compact’s elegance and said, “It is definitely blurring and mattifying, giving skin a soft, smooth, and natural finish.”

Hermès Plein Air Luminous Matte Skin Care Foundation

Our tester easily rated this a 10. “This foundation has a velvety, soft texture that spread beautifully. I prefer not having too much makeup on and was pleasantly surprised that one to two small drops was enough to provide light, even, and smooth coverage. It doesn’t feel like makeup and is more like a good moisturizer or skin care. It helps nourish and balance out my skin in addition to providing good coverage.”

This elegant stylo blurs, plumps, and adds shine to lips, making them look fuller and richer. Unlike some other red lipsticks on the market, which contain carmine, a pigment made from crushed female insects, this is a completely vegan alternative. Our tester called the color “bright and lovely” and loved “the smooth, hydrating feel of this lipstick versus traditional matte red lipsticks, which can feel drying.”

It Cosmetics Do It All Hydrating Sheer Tinted Moisturizer Balm

Another 10 rating came in for this product, which our tester said she would continue to purchase on her own. “It gave me even, dewy skin, but still looked natural, and I was able to use a brush (which doesn’t usually work on my dry skin) without any caking or visible dry texture. It also endured all day, which has not been the case with other balm foundations I’ve used.”

This kit gives a mix of nail stickers and press-ons, so that you can give yourself a manicure two ways. “These press-on nails look quite nice on,” said our tester. “I appreciated the thoughtfulness of creating nails you can reapply. If you take the sticker off, the nail is very ready to reuse, as if you never wore it.”

M.ph by Mary Phillips Underpainting Face Highlight and Contour Palette

Makeup artist Mary Phillips condensed her signature method of “underpainting”—thin lines of color corrector, contour, and highlighter that go on before foundation—into one palette. Our tester was nervous to try this at first, saying that it seemed like a lot of makeup and work. But she was pleasantly surprised to see how creamy and easy everything was to blend, and the palette gave her “the best no-makeup-makeup results.”

One/Size by Patrick Starrr Oil Sucker Liquid Blotting Paper Touch-Up Spray

“This definitely works,” said our tester. This touch-up spray acts like an oil-blotting sheet, instantly giving a blurred, matte finish that won’t disturb makeup, thanks to ingredients like ginseng and hydroclay to help absorb shine. She added, “I spritzed it after putting on my makeup and it kept my face looking cool and polished longer on a scorching day. It’s nice to be complimented on how un-sweaty you look!”

A shiny, chrome manicure used to be exclusively reserved for nail salons, but now, you can get the same effect at home, so long as the polish directly underneath is water-based. “The foam applicator is easy to use, and you don’t need to dab on much powder for that shiny chrome effect,” our tester shared. “The chrome effect even transformed an unflattering polish color into one I love wearing.”

If a wax, gel, and brow pencil had a throuple, it would make a weird movie but a great product—like this stick. Our tester gave it a 10, saying, “Love! Given my wimpy brow situation, I was a bit terrified of how thick this eyebrow pencil was. But, to my shock, it worked perfectly! I applied the chubby pencil against the natural direction of hair growth, then used the spoolie on the opposite end to shape the brows. It created a thick, natural-looking brow with a fluffy feathered effect that was even better than a tint because of the added texture.”

Laura Geller Anti-Hot Flash Setting Spray

Whether it’s because of a sweaty day or a hot flash, this spray keeps makeup in place for up to 18 hours and gives a light matte finish that doesn’t leave your skin feeling Saran-wrapped. Our tester said, “This is worth it because it truly keeps my makeup in place after a humid commute to work, all day in the office, and even throughout the night. It is light, non-greasy, and one spray goes a long way.”

It’s not a cream; it’s not a powder; it’s a cream-owder? Whatever it is, our tester liked it, marveling over this blush’s initial mousse cream texture that transforms with a light touch into a pigmented powder. “The product is great because it’s super pigmented but buildable. It blended right into my skin and gave me a slightly sun-kissed look. The texture makes it different than any other blushes I’ve tried.”

Many eye shadows tend to crease or fade after a few hours. With skin care ingredients like hyaluronic acid and ceramides, along with jojoba esters to help with hold, this one combines longwear power with a luxurious texture. “This was a super pigmented formula. The texture is velvety, which is really nice for an eyeshadow.”

Tilt Beauty The Easy Way Hydrating Satin Lipstick

An oversized grip and ergonomic design won this lipstick a “Certified Ease of Use” designation from the Arthritis Foundation. On top of that, the formula is silky and pigmented. Our tester called it a great lipstick with lasting color: “It’s super easy to use, glides smoothly onto the lips, and best of all, the top is magnetic and snaps on easily—especially convenient when you’re on the go.”

Not merely a foundation, this product melts into the skin like a serum and offers some additional protection benefits too, by being noncomedogenic and non-acnegenic. It wards off bacteria and breakouts with purslane and lactobacillus ferment. “When applying the rest of my makeup routine over top, the foundation not only remained fresh but did not formulaically clash with anything else I used. It doesn’t leave the skin oily, but just slightly glowy, and lasted well into the evening,” our tester said.

Urban Decay Lip Toy Staining Brush Lip Liner

The product compelled our tester to announce her love in all caps. It looks like a regular lip liner but also imparts a pretty, enduring stain. She did note that the thin brush applicator takes a little time to get the hang of, but after you do, it “stays the whole night!”

Victoria Beckham Colour Wash Bronze Water Tint

It was a great color match and smoothed out my freckly, uneven skin tone without settling into laugh lines. Vitamin E nourishes, while an aldehyde ingredient called hexanal helps to strengthen the keratin. Our tester used this on her nails after a gel manicure gone awry and “was genuinely surprised by how well it strengthened and protected them. They look as good as new.”

#6745. The 83 Most Innovative Beauty Products of 2026

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

As Mariah Carey once sang, “It’s time.” Each year, there are hundreds of new beauty products that promise to illuminate, brighten, slough, moisturize, hydrate, densify, and drench every inch of our bodies, from head to toe. But how good are they actually? And how special are they? Are the promises real? To find out, we put hundreds of products to the test.

After testing and combing through hundreds of entries for our 2026 Future of Beauty Awards, we found true innovators and standouts in skin care, hair care, body care, and color cosmetics, all rated highly by our editor testers. There are perfect 10s in every category, and we read many effusive reviews—from an NAD+ moisturizer that made someone a believer, to a top coat that made one tester’s nails glisten like diamonds, to one of the first sunscreens to use the new SPF filter, to a moisturizer meant to combat the effects of GLP-1s. See all 83 of our favorites below.

Bloomeffects Black Tulip RegeneLift Eye Serum

This eye serum may be lightweight, but thanks to ingredients like peptides and bakuchiol, our tester said it instantly smooths and hydrates, leaving the eye area brighter and more lifted. “My favorite part is the metal applicator. It feels cool on the skin, which is perfect for tired mornings,” she said. Another tester loved the milky texture, noting that it absorbed quickly without pilling under makeup.

Dr. Dennis Gross DRx BioRejuvenation Serum

Powered by a precise ratio of amino acids commonly found in collagen, this serum is designed to help firm, tighten, and visibly lift. Our tester applied the serum around her mouth and noticed that area looked progressively smoother over time. She said the silky texture and pleasant scent were an added bonus, making the twice-daily ritual feel a little more indulgent.

Dr. Diamond’s Metacine Liplift Hyaluron Peptide-6 BioFill Lip Treatment

Inspired by the in-office treatments that made Jason Diamond, MD, a favorite among A-listers like Kim Kardashian and Katy Perry, this lifting serum is designed to deliver fuller, smoother lips without the sting of traditional lip plumpers. The peptide-powered formula helps hydrate, soften fine lines, and enhance definition. Our tester said, “It has a lightweight, silky texture that makes it easy to wear on its own or layered over lip color.”

Dr.Reju-All Advanced PDRN Rejuvenating Lip Serum

Your answer to kissably soft, smooth, and supple lips arrives in this rich formula that looks like a gloss, with ingredients that read like your favorite facial serum: skin-barrier-strengthening PDRN, plus lactic acid and hibiscus enzymes for gentle exfoliation. “I love that the formula looks like a regular gloss, even though it’s repairing and soothing my lips underneath the sheen,” our tester shared.

Exoceuticals Exo Elevate Skin Longevity Complex Moisturizing Cream

Instead of a surplus of single-purpose formulas, what your skin needs is a moisturizer that treats everything effectively. Here it is: a formula that combines exosomes, peptides, fatty acids, and more to restore, strengthen, and balance skin. Our tester said: “After a week of using this, I started to really notice an improvement in some of my lines and the overall firmness of my skin. I definitely haven’t used a moisturizer before that had so many active ingredients.”

Expanding its Soy Cleansing empire, Fresh’s new balm has a texture that starts as jelly, becomes an oil, and then liquefies as you rinse it off. Soy protein is the star ingredient, adding moisture and boosting elasticity. Our tester felt that “the jelly did a great job of cleansing and removing my foundation while leaving it feeling clean and fresh, but not dry.” She also liked the tub format, noting, “I use a lot of facial cleansing oils, and this format felt innovative and easy to transport, which is not always the case with oils.”

“Gwyneth, I was not familiar with your game!” our tester raved. “Before testing this product, I read that it would lessen the appearance of my fine lines within a month. I was skeptical, but it really works!” This cream contains plant peptides and a precursor to NAD+, a powerful coenzyme that many celebrities use to biohack, as it purportedly helps stimulate mitochondria. “I have tried so many face creams over the years, but this is the only cream I think has actually made a difference.”

“My under-eyes looked about 20 percent brighter as a result of using the product,” enthused our tester after just a week of consistent use. The silver applicator dispenses one cool dose of two different types of vitamin C and hyaluronic acid to smooth, moisturize, and deliver radiance to the under-eye area.

La Roche-Posay Hyalu B5 Suractivated Rich Cream Jar

“Replumping” power is one of the most intriguing aspects of this moisturizer. In a study of GLP-1 users who used both this cream and its sister serum, they reported smoother and more radiant skin, perhaps thanks to the combination of multiple hyaluronic acids and ectoin, an ingredient that prevents water loss. Our tester said, “I liked using this cream first thing in the morning. It absorbs right away and provides quick moisture, which I found worked well under makeup.”

Manyo Glutathione 7 Dark Spot Serum Patch

Going beyond an under-eye or even a pimple patch, this is a patch—shaped like clouds, hearts, or stars, natch—that can go anywhere on your face to hydrate and reduce dark spots using glutathione and niacinamide. Our tester said, “My under-eyes are slightly less dark. But I enjoyed the cooling effect of these more than anything.”

People sometimes associate luxe face balms with granola-ish or sensuous formulations, rather than real science. But this one corrals powerful ingredients like vitamin C, resveratrol, and fisetin, a flavonoid that recent studies have shown can help clear out aged or dormant fibroblasts. “I really liked the product. I expected it to seize up or react strangely with my melange of other products, but in fact it seemed to lock them in to help them better absorb. It actually made my already oil-slicked skin feel less greasy. The next day, my naturally dry skin felt and looked hydrated.”

Medik8 Exo-PDRN Prismatic+ Vegan PDRN Serum With Triple Exosome Complex

PDRN and exosome treatments are both popular in Korea, and this serum is a vegan-friendly version of them, aimed at helping rejuvenate and moisturize skin. “I got a lot of sun over the summer, and this has helped bring my skin back to life! It has made it plumper and super hydrated,” our tester said.

For those who prefer their pimple patches on the subtle side, the reigning patch brand has developed a new invisible bandage. Our tester is a frequent pimple patch wearer and liked that this clear version discouraged skin picking and sealed out bacteria, so that her zits could flatten in just a few hours.

Olay Regenerist Lifting and Sculpting Face and Neck Treatment

A face sculptor and moisturizer in one, this rollerball dispenses a mix of collagen peptides and NAD+. The cooling applicator allows you to glide it across your neck, décolletage, and the lymph nodes for a sculpting effect. “I primarily used this on my neck and jawline and areas I don’t pay enough attention to. My skin looked plumper and smoother after use,” our tester said.

Like a shot of espresso for your face, this tingling cream mask has caffeine to depuff and peptides to add radiance. “The Caffeine Reset definitely feels like an elevated addition to my routine,” said our tester. “The formula felt cooling and comfortable on my sensitive skin, and I loved the packaging. The depuffing results weren’t dramatic for me, but I loved how it felt and think it’s the kind of product I’d especially reach for after a late night or early morning.”

This botanical-infused mineral sunscreen has an SPF-plus formula that visibly calms redness while protecting the skin. Our tester did want to flag that this is a capital-M mineral sunscreen, meaning that it has a thicker texture and requires a bit of rubbing. But it’s worth it, she said, because it provides sun protection that lasts longer than the formulas she usually uses.

Is it possible for a sunscreen to make you look glowy without being greasy? As it turns out, yes. This one comes in two shades and has a natural finish. Every swipe of this hydrating sunscreen gives skin a cast-free finish and has a water-burst-like texture, according to our tester, who loved how moisturizing it felt.

Dior Prestige La Crème Texture Essentielle

A combination of powerful, rare botanicals and a new “micro-filler” technology makes this cream especially luxurious. It contains peptides from Dior’s proprietary Rose de Granville, a hardy, resilient variety grown on the French cliffs. The brand’s micro-filler technology also glosses over texture and fine lines so that skin looks extra smooth. “Putting this on my chest made a weird shoulder crinkle disappear, so I can only imagine the possibilities that it could have for my face,” our tester said.

You may know Upneeq for its prescription eye drops that help open low-lying lids. Now, it has also made an over-the-counter eye cream to firm and brighten with vitamin C and peptides (though it does not help your eyes appear more open). “I love the way this cream feels going on,” our tester raved. “It’s so smooth, like butter, and absorbs easily.”

Our tester couldn’t stop marveling at how good her skin looked after using this cream. “It’s smooth and firmer, and I really see fewer fine lines. But my favorite thing about this cream is the way it melts in—I feel like I am taking a watering can to my face.” The formula is intensely hydrating, but its real breakthrough is that it’s made with Myrixin, a flavonoid complex with six different plant extracts that boasts Botox-like effects and helps smooth deep-set wrinkles.

Skorr Skin care Revive Exosome EGF Booster

Our tester didn’t really “believe” in serums. But this one, packed with lab-synthesized exosomes and growth factor technology, changed her mind. She said, “My skin immediately felt more plump, smooth, and visibly firm the first time I used it. I just looked in the mirror and thought to myself, Ooooh, my skin looks good! It had been a bit stressed from this summer’s erratic weather, and this serum seems to have not only repaired but erased some of that damage. It’s simply made my skin look so much better than it has in a while.”

MSK Labs The Night Affair Overnight Tanning Mask

Self-tanning meets skin care with this mask that combines a low level of DHA with erythrulose, a sugar that creates a sunless tanning effect, along with powerhouse ingredients like hyaluronic acid, niacinamide, lactic acid, and more. The combination had our tester marveling over the subtlety of the results. “It didn’t transfer on my pillow or make me look orange, and I woke up looking like I had freshly applied bronzer.”

SeneGence GLP-Complex with SenePlex+ and Exosomes

Don’t confuse this serum with a GLP-1—our tester found it to be the exact opposite. “Immediate plumping effects,” she said. “My cheeks were noticeably bouncier and had more volume.” Formulated with peptides, lipids, and plant-based exosomes, it’s an all-in-one serum that helps to protect, moisturize, and boost hydration. “I tried this along with several other serums and found it was the one I constantly gravitated toward. The feel is so hydrating and velvety, and it has a light, fairly neutral, non-medicinal smell.”

Biodance Caviar PDRN Liposome Bubble Booster

Feel like you’re moisturizing with champagne using this serum, which dispenses a hydrating essence with a unique, foamy effervescence. Unlike real champagne, it’s not sticky or drying; the bubbles are thanks to liposomes containing peptides and ceramides. Our tester said, “This is the kind of product that encapsulates modern K-beauty. The bubbly foam format was so fun, and my skin was moisturized too, with a softness that lasted all day.”

Lancôme Absolue Longevity MD Reset Face Cream

The brand partnered with Timeline, a longevity supplement brand, to create one of the first skin care ranges using Mitopure, a postbiotic that is alleged to trigger mitochondrial renewal. Our tester said that this cream felt supremely luxurious. “It was creamy and silky and had a faint, ‘fancy skin care’ scent. In the morning, I had soft, hydrated skin.”

Neutrogena Clear Face Sunscreen for Acne-Prone Skin Broad Spectrum SPF 60 with BEMT

This is one of the first U.S. sunscreens to contain BEMT, the first new SPF ingredient approved by the FDA in more than two decades, and our tester noticed the innovation immediately. “I liked that the consistency is quite thin and isn’t sticky—that’s my favorite for an under-the-makeup sunscreen. It was very easy to apply too.”

Sulwhasoo UV Wise On-the-Go Sun Cushion SPF 50+

Cushion compacts, a format that K-beauty often uses for foundation, are now getting the sunscreen treatment. In contrast to cream formats, the compact hides a broad-spectrum SPF 50 that you apply with a few taps of the cushion. Our tester loved the blendability of this formula and said the compact allowed for touch-ups throughout the day. “It was so moisturizing, and so much more efficient and less messy to apply SPF this way.”

Shiseido Vital Perfection Intensive SculptDefine Serum

Scientists at Shiseido created the D-AminoLift Complex, a new blend of amino acids that bolster collagen. With watercress to boost radiance and reduce sallowness, plus niacinamide and safflower red to help address oxidative stress, the serum is designed to plump and sculpt at the same time. Our tester said, “This texture has a velvety feel. It left my skin feeling renewed, moisturized, and really, really soft.”

If you want curl definition without sacrificing movement, this medium-hold gel is made for you. Our testers found that a thin layer of this lightweight jelly encouraged curls to clump together beautifully and tamed frizz for hours, all while leaving their hair soft, shiny, humidity-resistant, and touchable. Even better, the results held up beyond wash day. “This gel keeps my curls defined from first-to-second-day hair,” our tester shared, adding, “Usually it’s a mess by day two.”

SH-RD Beauty Corp. Exosome Root Awakening Concentrate

With 50 billion exosomes in a bottle, this professional-grade scalp serum is a huge boon for hair loss. Our tester says that she would buy this again. “I chopped off three inches a month ago and there seems to be about half an inch of growth already,” she said. One pro tip: Pair it with a DIY scalp massage to make sure it distributes evenly throughout your roots.

Kérastase Chronologiste Overnight Youth Serum

Shampooing and conditioning can only do so much to reverse damage—the real work happens post-shower. This serum works while you sleep, hydrating and fortifying strands while boosting shine. Our tester, who is very familiar with repair products, noticed that “it made my hair softer and appear less damaged” and declared it “more effective than others I have tried.”

I’ve been using this shampoo twice a week and my hair feels very clean and free of build-up. I love the scent, too. Even my hair dresser remarked on how shiny my hair is right now.”

#6744. Shooting Matt Damon in the Desert and Leaning Into Discomfort

It was a beautiful day to be indoors. In barren Lancaster, California, the temperature had hit the mid-90s, and the feels-like figure in my weather app topped triple digits. We were professional. We were stoic. But at a certain point, you just give in to the heat of the desert. At a certain point, you wilt a little.

Matt Damon, mercifully, never did. Damon may be the least-debated pick in GQ Men of the Year history—he’s still riding the billion-dollar wave of The Odyssey, one of the great cinematic accomplishments this century. Damon was also, by some distance, the most unbothered man on that desert set, and all while being the only one wearing heavenly black tailoring (a peak-lapel Norton & Sons jacket, and glorious mohair Celine pants). It was Bourne-esque. Odyssean, even! Three decades into being a film star, Damon has familiarity with endurance, both creative and literal. In his cover story, he walks our Frazier Tharpe through the physical cost of making Christopher Nolan’s epic, up to and including a night in Scotland on which he didn’t sleep at all. The piece is a portrait of a movie star who still treats the job as a job, most of all when it is inconvenient, unfair, or uncomfortable.

Discomfort has been something of a theme for me this year. I will confess that I knew 2026 would be an administrative ordeal from the moment I accepted this new role at GQ. There was a move from London to New York (including a cat, whose opinion was not consulted). There was a home to find. And there was the discovery that the land of the free has impressive disdain for a grown man with no credit score. None of it was enjoyable. All of it, I think, is good for me.

I have the good fortune to work with many brilliant editors and writers. A recurring note I like to give is: Where is the tension? Without tension, there’s no story. I am not a confrontational person, but I find confrontation on the page irresistible. I want you to stumble, in every issue of GQ, onto something uncomfortable, or uncomfortably new. A magazine that agrees with you from cover to cover is a magazine that you have already read. And I would hate to do that to you.

This issue does not let you off lightly. After some life advice from an honest-to-God astronaut who went deeper into space than anyone that’s ever lived, you’ll read about Chris Rock’s unvarnished thoughts on divorce, discomfort, and reclamation, and Andrew Garfield’s very real anxieties in playing Sam Altman, the complicated man who’s been described as the Oppenheimer of our time. From there, we hang out with the men risking the unknown to get hot on peptides, which is, for better or worse, the defining male-vanity story of 2026.

A few months ago I started putting my email address in these letters (adam@gq.com). You’ve been generous. You’ve been thoughtful. A handful of you have been unhinged. But one note showed up way more than I thought it would: NO POLITICS, PLEASE.

I understand the impulse. I know These Modern Times are stressful, and wouldn’t it be nice if GQ were a place of apolitical tranquility? Alas. GQ has always taken men seriously as full human beings, and full human beings can handle tough conversations, opposing viewpoints, and a little gray area. There’s nothing noble about steering clear of the uncomfortable.

Which brings me to our special package on Cornell, anchored by a dispatch from campus by Savannah Sobrevilla. It is about an alleged sexual assault. It is also about the men on the sidelines of sexual assault. Some of the young men we meet through the story are students who are earnestly and inelegantly trying to grapple with how serious the alleged crimes are, with the crude and cruel culture that surrounds them, and with what role, exactly, they play in exacerbating that culture—or could play in diminishing it. It’s not easy reading, but not everything should be. ✦

A version of this story originally appeared in the December 2026 issue of GQ with the title “A Paean to Discomfort.”

#6743. How Often Should You Be Cleaning Your Leather Furniture? It's More Often Than You Think

Every item on this page was hand-picked by a House Beautiful editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Pictured above: A leather seat in a space designed by Jill Tran of Tran + Thomas Design Studio.

No one invests in a piece of leather furniture with plans to replace it again in just a few years. After all, a full-grain leather sofa can go decades without cracking, and minor scuffs simply add to the patina.

But the key lies in how well you care for your leather furniture. Leather may be lower maintenance than fabric upholstery, but it still needs occasional TLC to last.

We reached out to textile experts for tips on how to extend the life of leather furniture. Here's how often you should clean and condition these pieces, and how to tell the difference between normal patina and damage that needs attention.

Lisa Beach is the director of operations at BenchMade Modern, a furniture company that specializes in leather. According to her, cleaning your leather furniture regularly protects it from damage.

She recommends vacuuming your leather furniture and wiping it down with a dry cloth any time you notice dust, pet hair and dander, or crumbs starting to accumulate. This can range from every one to two weeks, depending on how often you use the furniture. The goal is to prevent these particles from settling into the furniture and possibly scratching the surface.

In general, she warns against using a damp cloth during these quick and frequent cleanings. "For semi-aniline or protected leathers, you may wipe down your furniture every few months with a damp cloth, but be sure to follow with a dry cloth to remove any excess moisture," she says. "Natural full-grain leathers are more absorbent, so we don't recommend using damp cloths or moisture unless you need to clean a spill or stain."

Leather furniture also benefits from a deeper clean with a specialized pH-balanced product every few months.

Shawn McGowen is the CEO of Leather Honey, a family-owned company that has been producing leather care products since 1968. He suggests blocking off time for a deep clean every three to six months, and following it up with a leather conditioner.

Leather naturally ages and develops a patina over time. McGowen says patinaed leather shows off a slightly darker color, softer sheen and smoother feel. You'll first notice this starting to develop in the places you sit and rest your arms most often.

"This is where your natural body oils slowly burnish the leather’s surface, giving that lived-in look that people seek after," he says. "On the flip side, damage often occurs due to neglect and looks more like cracking, flaking, stiffness, or wrinkling that doesn’t go away with cleaning. Patina comes from properly using your leather while damage comes from neglect."

Note that leather furniture can fade instead of darken with age if it sits in a particularly sunny room. However, this doesn't mean you need to keep the curtains drawn or move your leather furniture to a darker corner. McGowen says that at-home recoloring balms or leather dyes are very easy to apply to small areas.

Even if you're a very determined DIYer, it's important to know when it's best to reach out to a leather care professional to restore your furniture. Otherwise, you may end up doing more harm than good.

"When leather becomes too far gone and starts to show signs of neglect or wear such as tears, holes, large areas of discoloration, or stains, that is when the DIY becomes more difficult and potentially a waste of time," McGowen says. "These should often be handled by professional leather care specialists because they have years of experience in color matching and upholstery, and have a wide range of tools needed for larger restoration projects."

#6741. Are Young Men the New Kings of the Swing Vote?

Two years ago, it seemed that the young men of Gen Z were shaping up to be hardcore conservatives. More than half of them voted for Donald Trump’s second presidency, refuting the idea that young people tend to lean progressive. Liberal media panicked about finding “a Joe Rogan of the left” who could reach them, and conservatives took a victory lap. “Gen Z Has Turned Right!” Fox News’s Greg Gutfeld announced, citing a report from the Harvard Kennedy School that highlighted, among other insights, the ways social media has led to an increase in conservatism among young folks. A Turning Point USA Poll from around the same time declared Generation Z “the most conservative generation in decades.”

On Tuesday night, though, as a blue tidal wave flipped states from Iowa to Texas, it was young men who comprised a critical segment of the new Democratic coalition. As reported by CIRCLE, a non-partisan research organization at Tufts University, white men between the ages of 18 to 29 voted blue by a 10-point margin; in 2024, exit polling indicated that 56% of all young men voted for Donald Trump. Even more remarkably, young people across the board voted for Democratic congressional candidates by a 40% margin, according to data from NBC.

“In the wake of 2024, there was this whole catastrophizing panic,” says Aidan Kohn-Murphy, the founder of Gen Z for Change (formerly known as TikTok for Biden) and a prominent political influencer. “Oh my God, everything we thought about Gen Z is wrong. It's actually a conservative generation. And no, that's not what was happening. I mean, a huge part of Gen Z just didn't show up to vote because they didn't feel like they had a reason.”

For Democratic strategist Eric Stern, connecting with men on an emotional level was critical in getting them out to vote. Stern has worked on Ruben Gallego and John Fetterman’s campaigns; his political ad agency, FIGHT, has produced spots for Mayor Zohran Mamdani, Senator-Elect Abdul El-Sayed, and Governor-Elect Gina Hinojosa, to name a few. This election cycle, Stern sought to change voters’ perception of what a Democrat can look like.

“Our most notable ad of the general election was Bob [Brooks] standing in front of a literal dumpster fire and then putting it out because he's a firefighter,” he said. Brooks, now the Representative-elect in the Lehigh Valley, flipped his seat on Tuesday. “A ton of Latino men are really into boxing and MMA fighting, and so Ruben [Gallego] held events around boxing,” Stern told me. For Texas’ Hinojosa, who'll become the state’s first Democratic governor in 35 years when she's sworn in in January, that meant running an ad during Mexican boxing champion Canelo Álvarez's match against Christian Mbilli, an event with a massive Latino viewing audience.

Still, Kohn-Murphy and Stern both argued that the main issue on the ballot this cycle was affordability. “If you look at polling, far and away the top issue for voters is cost of living,” Stern said. “And so you can run a million ads saying that ‘Jane Doe is for they/them,’ but it's not going to resonate with people in the same way if they can't afford to keep their lights on.”

When I asked Republican political consultant Kirsten Kukowski about young men voting blue, she brought up affordability too. After all, nearly nine months into the Iran War, a barrel of oil still costs $100. “Men and young men voted with their bank accounts,” she offered. “And for the last several years, that's how they've been voting. And I think that's how Donald Trump won that group two years ago, because he was talking about affordability and inflation.”

Kukowski, the former Communications Director for the Republican National Committee, now runs her own firm, K2 and Company, out of Minneapolis. “At some point here, people expect results,” she continued. “People expected results from Donald Trump, and they didn't see that. So now we're on the clock again for another two years.”

#6740. Have Yourself A Chrissy Little Christmas

Chrissy Teigen is trading a picture-perfect holiday for homemade ornaments, flour-dusted counters, and a verrry full house.

Christmas for Chrissy Teigen, cookbook author and founder of the food and bakeware brand Cravings by Chrissy Teigen, means getting the whole crew together: her husband, award-winning artist and producer John Legend; their four kids; three dogs; a bearded dragon; and a parrot. There’s a lot going on under one roof.But Chrissy handles chaos with ease. These days, she’s more focused on making memories with Luna, 10, Miles, 8, Esti, 3, and Wren, 3, than on making everything perfect.Much of that joy happens in the kitchen, where Chrissy has built a career writing three best-selling cookbooks and launching a line of baking mixes in unexpected flavors, like snickerdoodle with miso-honey sugar.The family travels often, but Chrissy is looking forward to staying put this winter. It’ll give her more space to embrace the spontaneity she’s craving this year—the kind you can find only when you’re home for the holidays.

Chrissy Teigen: Oh, Halloween. We have a big kids’ Halloween party here at the house. We pick a theme and run with it. This year we picked Coraline.

We love crafting so much. I wanted to stay home for the holidays this year because I need all my children to start hand-making their ornaments and putting them on the tree. I’ve entered this time in my life where I want Christmas to be completely whimsical and over the top. I want to have a cute, colorful, chaotic Christmas this year. I want to thread the popcorn onto the string and have the most chaotic kids’ Christmas tree ever. And I can’t wait for that.

Cooking, of course. Scalloped potatoes in my house are very, very holiday. And my mom comes to town. She lives in Thailand, so we love getting the whole family together. And there’s a lot of football. John’s family loves it, and there’s usually a big Ohio State game around Thanksgiving that they all go crazy for. They go just as nuts for the Christmas game, too. It’s very much the whole family on the floor of my house, watching everything together, and it’s beautiful.

It will be filled with so much joy, but it’s going to be an emotional one for me, of course. To have that big yellow chair that my dad loved so much, empty...I don’t exactly know what to expect from me emotionally, but I can imagine it will be all the things that make the holidays so complex: sad and wonderful and special all at the same time.We will bake, with love, the potatoes Dad loved so, so much. We will clean the greens the way Granny (on John’s side) told us to. I’m sure our eyes will well up. It’ll be a testament to how much they meant to us all.

It is, but that recipe has always haunted me. My mom is impossible to get a recipe from. I think a lot of moms do this on purpose: You ask them to share a recipe, and something’s missing, or they leave out one little thing.I’ve checked in about this one so many times. She’s made it twice a year my whole life, and every time I ask about a detail—whether she uses MSG or when she adds the pepper—I get a different answer. So I’m not even sure I have the right recipe in my cookbook. Whenever she makes it, something is different. You could call it a mom’s touch, but I think she’s leaving out an important ingredient because she wants hers to be the best.

We do Tyler Florence’s beef Wellington every year. It looks impressive, but it’s quite simple, and it’s never gone wrong for me. When I cut into it, the color is beautiful, and it’s juicy. Over the years, I’ve learned how to keep the pastry crisp, even on the bottom where the meat juices collect. It’s a flawless recipe, and the peppercorn sauce is amazing. It pleases everyone, and the kids can cut shapes out of the puff pastry. For sides, we have the scalloped potatoes, green beans, and carrots.

My girls are always in the kitchen with me—I’m not allowed to cook without them. I love it, even though it slows things down. Esti is kind of the boss of the house right now, and she gets nervous that if she goes upstairs to shower, we’ll finish the recipe without her. We make sure she’s back in time to cook the recipe from beginning to end. I expect Esti and Luna will do most of the cooking for Christmas dinner.

No, they’re more likely to spill things. The living room is always covered in spills. I often put my drink on the floor because the table is too far away, and the boys knock it over with their feet.

Miles is our “won’t eat anything” guy. He’s eight now, and he’s been picky since he started eating solid food. He mostly likes chicken nuggets, fries, hamburgers, and pizza. Esti’s tastes change quickly. She might want apples with every dinner, then decide she doesn’t like them—and be like, “I never liked apples. Are you crazy?”Wren is our tank. He’ll eat anything. He gets almost catatonic when he’s eating, so he forgets it could make him sick.Luna knows how to make foods taste better. If something has a burnt corner, for example, she’ll cut it off or find another way to improve it. I think that’s really special.

I remember not liking raw tomatoes or avocados when I was young, even though I love them now, so I’m not too worried about how the kids’ tastes will change. They’re kids, and they’re told what to do all day. Food can be one way they speak up and feel like their opinions matter. I get that—it must be hard to be told what to do from morning to night. As long as they’re full and not waking up hungry, I’m fine.

Christmas morning is an all-day experience for us. Once my kids open a present, they want to play with it, so they take their time instead of rushing through the gifts. I used to have so many rules and run around stressing about the wrapping and recycling. Now I just relax and let the day unfold.

I love prepping for Christmas the night before, especially when it’s a secret, and I’m in charge. I’ll make hot cocoa bombs. In the morning, the kids just have to pour hot milk over them. And we’ll have pancakes, bacon, and Mrs. Butterworth’s syrup, get a sugar rush, and then hopefully have like a nice midday crash together.

Luna has always wanted to be in on the secret. I worried that, as my oldest, she might feel like she was missing out on the Christmas magic, but she loves helping make it special for her younger siblings. Last year, I asked if she wanted to stay up all night with me, and she loved that more than anything.She and Esti also love making stockings for the dogs, the bearded dragon, and now the parrot. We had lots of animals growing up, and I remember my mom taking me to PetSmart to pick out little gifts for our bird. I made sure every beating heart in the house got celebrated, and now the kids love doing that, too.

The bearded dragon toy industry is small. Last year we got little inflatable pools, and there are dried insects, as gross as they are. And a little Santa hat. He’s happy with that. I used to make bird toys with my dad for our parrot, so getting things for Audrey reminds me of that time with him.

Without a doubt, and I’m putting Luna in charge. Carrot cake is our family’s favorite. It was my wedding cake, and it’s John’s family’s favorite, too. There’s so much warmth in it: It has spices and real carrots, and it’s sticky, decadent, and delicious. It’s our holiday pleaser.In addition to our carrot cake kit, I hope we can one day offer a kit with lots of elements for people to decorate a cake and have fun with. The holidays are all about slowing down and getting your hands into something, and it doesn’t hurt if you can eat it after, too.

No, but I loved baking mixes, especially confetti cake mix. My dad had a big sweet tooth, and baking mixes and TV dinners were important to me. My mom’s food seemed too spicy or daunting when I was a kid, so my dad and I bonded over Hungry-Man dinners, Kid Cuisine, and Hot Pockets. Homemade food was mostly for the holidays.I think that’s why grocery stores still excite me. I take my kids shopping and love seeing what’s new—and finding foods that taste just like I remember, like frozen Salisbury steak. Those were the foods I grew up on, and I wanted to recreate them at home for my Cravings cookbooks. I’ve never looked down on those foods. They’ve stood the test of time for a reason. They’re nostalgic and wonderful.

#6738. Melitta Baumeister Is the Designer Keeping New York Weird

The bold designer is braving the New York fashion landscape with unique shapes and high concepts.

Only at a Melitta Baumeister presentation in New York would you find the artist Kembra Pfahler playing a tuba, Paola Castro doing backflips, and Colleen Litchfield sculpting clay on the floor with sneakers gliding by on motorized platforms with chunky tires. “I really loved the idea of just doing flips in your living room,” the designer tells ELLE of her event last month. Sitting in her Greenpoint atelier in Brooklyn, Baumeister is wearing one of her signature black pointy baseball caps covered in pleated fabric. It’s become such a trademark for her that the people who know her best barely recognize her without it.

But that’s not all she’s known for. Since launching her own fashion line over a decade ago, Lady Gaga and Rihanna have worn her work, and the label has become a favorite of fashion insiders, with over-the-top conceptual shows, directional collections sold at places like Dover Street Market, and sporty collaborations. Her latest foray is a second collaboration with Nike, which involved the aforementioned performance-art-centric presentation to launch her rework of the Tabi-silhouetted Rift. Her take is a chunky, clay-inspired wedge of a shoe with gasp-inducing details like eyeball motifs and puffy logos.

Baumeister’s work has a cult following because it puts a focus on handmade details in an unusual, raw way. But she also has always gravitated towards big, voluminous shapes that take up space in an uncompromising way, with an element of sportiness. At her New York Fashion Week debut, for instance, Paralympian sprinter Scout Bassett opened the show. Sculpted dresses with bell-shaped bottoms, architectural pointed blouses, and surreally satisfying pleated dresses were put on full display with banana-shaped pouches, spiked necklaces, and, of course, that signature hat done in various colors like lemon yellow.

“I think because there’s so much out there, I feel like making statements to show creativity,” Baumeister says. “I feel like that’s why you have to push because there’s just too much boring stuff out there.” The designer describes her house codes as O lines and A lines, always with a voluminous shape. “My approach is always to make a garment kind of feel three-dimensional. There might be a drape from the side view, but it’s all very considered. It comes from hand draping, working with the fabric, and seeing what the fabric can do with its structure.”

Born in a tiny town in the Black Forest in southern Germany, Baumeister went to tailoring school at the age of 16 before studying fashion design at the Pforzheim University of Applied Sciences. She later moved to New York City to study at Parsons in the MFA Fashion Design and Society program and officially founded her line when she graduated in 2014. In 2023, she won the CFDA/Vogue Fashion Fund, and in 2025, she won the National Design Award for Fashion Design.

Earlier this year, the Costume Institute at the Metropolitan Museum of Art acquired her “Venus” dress and exhibited it at the “Costume Art” show. She works alongside her partner, Michal Plata, who also serves as the brand’s art director. The city continues to inspire her: “the energy is so special and so strong here that I feel like it kind of gives me that sort of harshness in design,” she says. “We’re very bold and kind of very daring. I don’t think I would do the same in Germany in my hometown.”

Though many of her pieces are designed in charcoal black, when she’s not working in neutrals, it’s neons. “I really want color to feel strong and bold rather than kind of too soft or too safe,” she says. Color is a storytelling method for her, and the brighter colors also represent energy. Baumeister continues to pull from the earliest days of her brand, like when she created clothing out of poured silicone. “I’m still very inspired by that, because it was not about caring about wearability, it was more like an art piece than clothing.” One of her brand missions is to show that garments don’t have to be traditionally sewn, or conventionally shaped. Another pillar for her is the accessories and footwear. “A bag talks so much about the character of a woman, and the shoe as well, because you can feel more feminine or more masculine or more in a certain way,” she says.

At the moment, she’s busy working on her own line (with a potential return to fashion week on her mind) after finishing her latest Nike collaboration, which just launched. The Nike collaboration includes clothing in addition to the shoes, like sculpted sweatpants accented with nose-shaped keyrings. “I always think shoes or sneakers nowadays look so much that they’re coming out of a machine and no one touched them and they’re just perfect,” she says of the collection. “I wanted to highlight that things are made by hand and there is a lot of hand work also in shoes.”

For the occasion, she actually hand-sculpted the shoes with clay in her atelier to form the final mold. That’s part of the reason why the presentation was so theatrical and involved a live sculptor: “I wanted to show how things are made. I want people to come to the thought that there’s no God-given talent, and you’re not born as a final product. Things have to be built from the ground up. There’s always another thing that you have to work on.”

That’s what makes her work so exciting. Baumeister is still most interested in what fashion can become when it refuses to conform to the typical shape.

#6737. 13 Rugby Shirts Under $150 for Credible Old-Money Cosplay

Historically, rugby shirts are a somewhat conflicted character in the never-ending tale of menswear shirting. On one hand, they’re a prep staple on par with OCBDs, loafers, and boat shoes. On the other hand, they were quite literally designed to handle the rigors of full-contact rugby and were later embraced by folks who proudly call themselves dirtbags.

There’s an interesting duality to rugby shirts: they can feel twee or they can feel traditionally sporty. Thanks to this polarity, it makes it even easier to fit into your wardrobe. Regardless of which camp you fall in, rugby’s crush. And thanks to a recent resurgence, we thought it would be a good idea to highlight some rugby shirts under $150.

Not only are they traditionally as comfortable as any polo you own, they’re also refined enough to take into stuffier settings, rugged enough to handle some serious outdoor exploration and flexible enough to wear from Friday to Monday morning (but don’t do that, probably). And while we love an expensive version of, well, anything, we also love it when we can find some killer alternatives for prices we’d like to think of as reasonable. There wasn’t a paucity of options either, so we rounded up 13 of our favorites, right below.

One of the least expensive options on this list is also one of the best—the loose, shorter cut makes wearing it with jeans, some chinos or virtually anything else in your closet as easy as any polo you have in the arsenal.

J.Crew has a few louder options in the catalog, but the pared-down, solid-bodied iterations are even more affordable and play a supporting role with as much skill and charisma as your favorite character actor.

There is an actual science to color combinations, and Gramicci deserves an Einstein Award for this one.

When done right, a rugby can be one of the best outdoors shirts on the market, and this one is just that: super comfortable, a little hardy and delightfully reminiscent of the ones you would've seen in Camp 4.

There's simply no way that the company founded by an original Camp 4 denizen could ever make a rugby not worthy of a Half Dome assault, and it's also improbably still under $150.

Alex Mill makes a few killer monochromatic, garment-dyed variations of this striped number, but this one is more fun than those and we could all use a little bit of fun when the days are as short as they are now.

It's odd to call something a rugby if it doesn't have a contrasting placket, but this one gets a pass because everything else checks out and it's also made out of a hardy-ass jersey fabric.

One of the best rugby shirts we've found also happens to meet the rigorous price qualifications of this list—and now it's half-off—and it's not all that often that the best of anything is also a value play.

Putting "Iconic" in the name is one of those things that can backfire on you if it doesn't live up to the billing, but this one does and remains both a standard of the genre and, against all odds, under $150.

Very few brands appreciate the rugby—or the possibilities that exist under the rugby banner— more than 18 East, and not only is the Harold reimagining the notion of stripe spacing within the genre, but it's also the only short-sleeve option on this list.

This one is going to keep working its way into lists as long as its available because sometimes a brand just nails it. In this case, the cocktail of perfectly-faded brown and black is radiating Leatherheads (complimentary) and the cut is the comfy kind that can layer on either side.

#6735. Jennifer Aniston on ‘The Rachel’ Revival, the Bob She Regrets, and LolaVie’s New Hairspray

Every item on this page was chosen by an ELLE editor. We may earn commission on some of the items you choose to buy.

Jennifer Aniston may just be hair’s ultimate cultural icon. Her Friends character Rachel Green inspired one of the most coveted hairstyles of the ’90s: “The Rachel,” a voluminous, layered cut that sent countless women to salons in search of hair that looks as effortlessly chic as Aniston’s did on-screen. The irony? She remembers the style as anything but effortless. “I remember how much work that original haircut was!” Aniston tells ELLE exclusively.

Today, hair trends have shifted toward styles that feel more relaxed, lived-in, and less polished—including the modern version of “The Rachel” that features longer and softer layers. There’s also a greater emphasis on hair that looks and feels healthy, with movement and texture taking precedence over a stiffly styled finish. Even Aniston prefers the “undone” approach. “I don’t love anything that makes your hair stiff or crunchy,” she says. That philosophy is at the heart of the latest addition to her hair-care brand, LolaVie: the Weightless Medium Hold Hairspray. “We wanted to create a product that gives you hold without sacrificing the feel of your hair,” she adds.

Powered by adaptive peptides and a trio of tropical fruit-derived actives, the superfine spray sets your style in place while leaving behind touchable softness and a healthy-looking shine. The formula even makes second-day styling a breeze, with 450°F heat protection and 72-hour frizz protection.

In an interview with ELLE ahead of the product’s launch today, Aniston reveals her hair-care routine, her bob era, why she envies Sandra Bullock, and her favorite rom-coms. Read the full conversation below.

I try to keep it pretty simple. I always start with a good shampoo and conditioner, and then choose products depending on what my hair needs that day. If I’m blow-drying, I go for heat protection and a little smoothing. If I’m going for a more natural style, it’s all about hydration and movement.

We wanted to create something that gives you hold without sacrificing the feel of your hair. The Weightless Medium Hold Hairspray helps keep your style in place while still allowing your hair to move and feel soft and touchable.

I’ve always been curious about advances in science and medicine, especially when it comes to taking care of yourself, but I’ve learned it’s important to be discerning about where you get your information. I always do my research and have trusted sources and experts that I turn to. Just because something is trending doesn’t mean it’s right for you—or anybody, for that matter.

There’s so much information and so many new treatments available, which is wonderful, but it can also feel overwhelming. I hope the future is about getting back to the basics: sleep, movement, nutrition, managing stress, and taking care of yourself consistently. Technology can be amazing, but it’s not a replacement for the fundamentals.

I love that people are still inspired by it. The modern versions feel a little more relaxed and lived-in, which I love.

Many times throughout the years. Hair is such a fun way to experiment with how you look and feel, and sometimes you just want a change. I remember a couple of times thinking, A bob would be fun, so I took the leap...and then immediately started to let it grow back out. What can I say? I’ve found what works for me.

Sandy has the thickest head of hair on a woman over 40 that I’ve ever seen in my entire life! I have such hair envy! She is also one of my biggest LolaVie supporters. She’s ordered every product available from day one and continues to do so. I know she loves me, but God knows she doesn’t have to do that.

There are so many good ones! When Harry Met Sally, of course, is the quintessential rom-com. Rob Reiner and Nora Ephron—nobody did it better. The Philadelphia Story is also a classic.

It’s bittersweet. I’m proud of the journey we’ve taken Alex on and very grateful to have been part of telling these stories with such an extraordinary cast and crew. Our writers did an incredible job wrapping up not only Alex’s journey but the other characters we’ve come to love. You’ll just have to wait and see!

This isn't merely an eminently wearable low-pro puffer—it's TWO eminently wearable low-pro puffers.

They don't make comfier Blazers than the SB iteration that's got a hit of ultra-responsive Zoom Air in the heel.

They're saying a more fall-coded red doesn't exist.

These can handle all kinds of footwear options, making them an ideal rotation player all winter long.

Sometimes it's just nice to get a pair of eminently wearable, wonderfully worn-in jeans for $50.

#6733. 7 Costco Foods You Should Buy Early For Thanksgiving—And 5 You Should Skip

Visit the warehouse before it becomes a madhouse.

It’s November, so it’s officially time to plan and shop for your Thanksgiving menu. And if you’re planning to feed a crowd and would prefer to stay within budget, it’s no surprise that Costco is one of the best places to stock up on ingredients. That is, if they’re even in stock.

The warehouse is more like a madhouse in the days leading up to major holidays. Shopping early allows you to check everything off your list and spares you a lot of stress later this month. However, not all ingredients are worth buying in advance.

I write about food and Costco for a living, and as much as I love the bulk warehouse, I can still be honest about what you shouldn’t buy. Here are the warehouse essentials I’d recommend buying early for your Thanksgiving dinner—and the things I’d skip.

Williams Sonoma suggests serving anywhere from half to an entire bottle of wine per guest at Thanksgiving. And if your local Costco sells booze, the warehouse often carries solid-quality bottles at a fraction of the retail price.

From gravy to stuffing, chicken stock serves as the foundation of some essential holiday foods. Kirkland Signature’s shelf-stable cartons are a great thing to buy ahead of time so you’re ready to go on Thanksgiving.

If you want to avoid washing your fine china and pressing linen napkins, disposable plates, silverware, and napkins are key. Costco is currently offering a wide range of options. Whether you want festive, fall-themed paper plates or elegant heavyweight plastic ones, you can save a lot of money on your tablescape by using Costco serving supplies.

If you’re baking desserts from scratch this year, Costco is the ideal spot to stock up on ingredients. From vanilla extract to pecans to cans of pumpkin puree, the warehouse carries everything you need in sizes large enough to feed your whole extended family.

Whether you’re serving them mashed, scalloped, or marshmallow-topped, you’ll most likely serve some sort of potato at your Thanksgiving feast. A 10-pound bag is one of those no-brainer Costco purchases that you can buy well before the holiday. Just keep the spuds in a cool, dark area until Thanksgiving.

If you’re starting Thanksgiving shopping early, some dairy products won’t last until the big day. Hard cheese is an exception. Costco sells huge blocks of cheddar and Parmesan that can stay unopened and fresh in the fridge for weeks. By the time Turkey Day rolls around, it’ll all be ready to go for homemade mac and cheese, scalloped potatoes, and appetizer spreads.

Butter belongs in virtually every dish on a traditional Thanksgiving menu. And these days, it’s not cheap at standard supermarkets. Thankfully, you can buy it in bulk at Costco. Kirkland Signature’s butter is just as good as brand-name, and it lasts for weeks unopened in the fridge.

Potatoes are sturdy and durable enough to sit in your pantry for weeks, but that doesn't apply to most other vegetables. Leafy greens, herbs, and pre-trimmed green beans will get nasty fast. I’d recommend skipping them on your initial trip and going back closer to the holiday to grab your vegetables.

When you’re shopping ahead of time, I’d recommend steering clear of most baked goods—especially bread—unless you plan to freeze them. Both dinner rolls and full-sized loaves will go stale before Thanksgiving. And, contrary to popular belief, you actually shouldn’t use stale bread in stuffing.

It should go without saying. If you buy milk for Thanksgiving recipes weeks ahead of time, it’ll end up more sour than unsweetened cranberry sauce.

I have mixed feelings about Costco’s pumpkin pie in general, specifically with the no-butter crust. That said, it also doesn’t hold up well if you buy it at least a week in advance. The custardy filling tends to leach moisture and give you the dreaded soggy bottom. If you insist on serving a Costco dessert this year, go for the pumpkin cheesecake instead and freeze it until Turkey Day.

This point comes with some caveats. Costco turkeys are undoubtedly a good deal, but buying them early is not always practical for some households. If you’re buying it ahead of time, the turkey should definitely be frozen to prevent spoilage. But unless you have plenty of freezer space (or a separate chest freezer), I wouldn’t recommend buying a turkey that far in advance.
